No DB stage without a slot; no CREATE DATABASE.

**Rule READ-2 (amended; balances).** The `BalanceOut` rows of a book are rebuilt from the book's hash-verified `Trace` (the `calc_trace` row of the as-of contract version, READ-1), not from `contract_version_balance`: every node whose id is `<measure>:<contract>@<entity>:<period_key>` — the stage-10 member-balance nodes `TraceBuilder.node(measure, subject_key = contract_entity_subject_key(contract, entity), period_key)` with `measure` one of the T-CON-09 balance measures `contract_liability`, `contract_liability_current`, `contract_asset`, `contract_asset_current`, `unbilled_receivable`, `accounts_receivable`, `refund_liability`, `return_asset`, `deposit_liability`, `customer_incentive_asset`, `consideration_payable`, `cost_asset_carrying`, `loss_provision` — contributes `columns["<measure>_txn"]` = the node's posted value (`format_money` text at the node's `params["minor_unit"]`, back to int minor units, XR-03) and, when the entity's functional currency is the transaction currency, `columns["<measure>_functional"]` (the engine's `_balances` rule; a foreign-currency entity's functional columns are stage 12's and are not rebuilt here — absent, never derived); `columns["entity"]`, `["txn_currency"]`, `["functional_currency"]` name the codes; the five measures the engine zero-fills for every member balance (`refund_liability`, `return_asset`, `deposit_liability`, `customer_incentive_asset`, `consideration_payable`) are zero-filled the same way for a (subject, period) that carries at least one member-balance node. One `BalanceOut(subject_key, period_key, columns, trace_nodes = measure → node id)` per (subject, period) found. `checkpoint_run` then requires, for every contract block that carries `balances` and each of its balance rows' entities, a rebuilt balance for `(<contract>@<entity>, the checkpoint's as_of period of that entity — FY<year>-P<month> under the MONTHLY January calendar, else refused)`; a missing one refuses by name. Cross-check: for each T-CON-09 row of the version whose subject's latest trace period equals that as_of period, every money column the row carries must equal the rebuilt value (int minor units; `*_functional` at the functional unit); a difference refuses by name naming the column — the row is never the source. Per-period balance persistence stays a post-rc candidate (ENG / DB lanes).

**FOLL-3 optional schema members (Q-11 ruling, D-98 54; dev-guide rev 1.26).** §9.5.3 `fx_rate_sets` gains `name?` (T-REF-10 label), `rule_sets` gains `example_cases?: [{name, input, expected_output}]` (T-REF-27 cases), §9.5.4 `judgements` gains `rationale?` (T-CON-19) — optional, never derived; the engine runner ignores them. Models: `FxRateSet.name`, `RuleSet.example_cases` (`RuleExampleCase(name, input, expected_output)`), `Judgement.rationale`. Converters: `fx_rate_set_request(rate_set)` uses `rate_set.name` and `adapt(fx_set)` refuses by name only when it is absent; `judgement_request(judgement, …)` uses `judgement.rationale`, `adapt(judgement)` refuses only when absent; a rule set with `example_cases` plans `create_config_test_case` per case (subject `rule_set_version`, `input` as the facts, `expected_output`) and `run_rule_set_version_tests` before `submit_rule_set_version` (handlers `rule_case`, `rule_tests`), and `adapt(rule_set_submit)` refuses only when the set carries no cases. `SCHEMA_GAPS` keeps the three refusals for keys without the members. None of the five keys carries the members; their native outcomes are unchanged. Fail-first: `.run/f-rps-e1/fail-first-balances-q12.log` (POS-CHK-012 end-of-p1 / end-of-p2 and POS-CHK-117 march-close refuse "carry balances … Q-12"; no trace-based reader) and the FOLL-3 refusals of §18.1 (judgement `rationale`, FX `name`, rule-set TESTED).

## 24. Codex native evidence on the trace-balances reader (712d8773 / 67aee4c6): a financial-reader defect — rule READ-2 amended again (D-98 72; docs first)

Supervisor's words, verbatim: Codex's first native evidence on the trace-balances reader (exact 712d8773 / runtime 67aee4c6; raw `2026-09-20-f-rps-read2-712d877-native.json` SHA256 `d5edb5d4…`, root-verified; original key SHA `ae5782cc…`; full report in progress) found a **financial-reader defect**: with POS-012 (both checkpoints), POS-117 march-close and RET-JS-03 shipment assembled / computed without altering inputs or oracles, all original native-engine balance checks pass, the native traces were serialized and hash-verified through the normal trace reader and supplied through inert TableSource rows to the reconstruction — and **RET-JS-03's USD refund liability 5,000.00 and return asset 2,800.00 (engine minor units 500000 and 280000) both reconstruct as 0.00**, with no reader error and no missing-balances refusal. Cause per Codex: the reader seeks only member-shaped `measure:contract@entity:period` nodes, while the producer's refund / return values live in component / obligation nodes; the reader zero-fills the missing fields. **Ruling D-98 72 (applies D-98 65):** the reader must reproduce the engine's own balance aggregation, not a node-name convention — a measure whose producer nodes live at component / obligation grain is read from those nodes and summed exactly as the engine's `_balances` rule sums them (same grain, same sign, same rounding to minor units); a measure with no producer node at any grain is a refusal by name; zero-fill is permitted only where the engine itself zero-fills (its five zero-filled measures), never for a measure the engine produced; and add a grain check so a group subject never appears as a member row (Codex is examining that separately). Fail-first with RET-JS-03 (expected 500000 / 280000 from the hash-verified trace; the current reader returns 0), then keep POS-012 / POS-117 / EX42 native outcomes unchanged; never derive a new oracle or alter a key — preserve the original 5,000 / 2,800 expectations and lineage. Q-12 / READ-2 acceptance stays OPEN.

Fail-first (`.run/f-rps-e1/fail-first-balances-d98-72.log`, at 9550d6e3): RET-JS-03 shipment computed by the engine, its ASC606 trace serialized with `trace_document` and read back hash-verified with `trace_from_row`, fed to `trace_balances` over inert `legal_entity` rows: the engine's `JS-03@JS-US FY2026-P03` carries `refund_liability_txn` 500000 and `return_asset_txn` 280000; the reader's row carries **0 and 0** with no refusal; and the reader emits **`CG-JS-03@JS-US`** — the group subject — as a member row (grain leak). The producer nodes are `refund_liability:CG-JS-03@JS-US/RETURN/JS-03/L1-MACHINES:FY2026-P03` (component grain) and `return_asset:JS-03/L1-MACHINES:FY2026-P03` (obligation grain), which the reader ignored.

**Rule READ-2 (amended, D-98 72 — the reader mirrors `erev_engine._balances`).** The book's `BalanceOut` rows are rebuilt from its hash-verified trace by the engine's own aggregation, in three grains:

1. **Member grain** — nodes `<measure>:<contract>@<entity>:<period>` whose measure is a T-CON-09 balance measure and whose contract component is a member of the group open the row `(<contract>@<entity>, period)` with `columns["<measure>_txn"]` = the node's posted value (`format_money` text at `params["minor_unit"]` → signed int minor units, XR-03), `columns["<measure>_functional"]` when the entity's functional currency is the transaction currency, `entity` / `txn_currency` / `functional_currency`, and `trace_nodes[measure]` = the node id — as `_balances` reads `st.member_balances`. **Grain check:** a `<X>@<entity>` node whose `X` is the group code is the group's own row and is never emitted as a member row; an `X` that is neither a member nor the group refuses by name.
2. **Component grain** — refund liabilities: nodes `refund_liability:<group>@<entity>/<KIND>/<source…>:<period>`; the row they belong to is `(<contract>@<entity>, period)` where the contract is the one the component's source names (D-90 `_source_contracts` grammar per KIND: `RETURN` and `VARIABLE_CONSIDERATION` → `<contract>/<obligation | element>`; `TERMINATION` → `<contract>/EV-<n>`; `CONCESSION` → its two contracts) together with the head when the head is not the group code — exactly one contract, else refused by name (the engine's `contract_of` maps each component to one contract); the entity is the head's `@<entity>`.
3. **Obligation grain** — return assets and the other targets the engine emits per obligation (`refund_liability.py` `Target(ctx.book_code, ob.contracting_entity, subject_key, measure, period, …)`): nodes `<measure>:<contract>/<obligation>:<period>`; the contract is the leading component; the entity is the obligation's contracting entity — read from the persisted `obligation_version` row of the version (`contracting_entity_id`), and, when that row is not in the source, from the head of the refund component node of the same trace whose source names that obligation; neither → refused by name (never guessed).

The summed measures (`refund_liability`, `return_asset`, `accounts_receivable`, `deposit_liability`, `customer_incentive_asset`, `consideration_payable`) attach only to a `(<contract>@<entity>, period)` row the member grain opened — the engine's `if found is None: continue`; several producer nodes for one (row, measure) are summed as signed int minor units and cite no node (`trace_nodes` names a node only when exactly one produced the column) — the engine's `if len(items) == 1`. The five zero-filled measures are zero-filled for every member row and nothing else is; a measure the engine produced is never zero-filled. The functional columns of a foreign-currency entity are stage 12's (`_functional_balances`) and stay absent. **Asserted measures:** `checkpoint_run` requires, for every contract block's balance row and every amount the row asserts, that the rebuilt `(<contract>@<entity>, as-of period)` row carries `<measure>_txn` (from a producer node or the engine's own zero-fill); a missing row or measure refuses by name (`<contract>@<entity> <period> <measure>`), never zero. The T-CON-09 cross-check of §21 stands. Correctness proof: for every checkpoint bundle of a set of balance-carrying keys (RET-JS-03 both checkpoints, POS-CHK-012 both, POS-CHK-117, the multi-member two-entity `ENT-PER-ENTITY-NETTING-IN-A-COMBINED-GROUP`, the cross-currency `ENT-CROSS-CURRENCY-PAIR-GBP-CONTRACT-USD-PERFORMER`, `CPC-CHK-120-MIXED-ORDINARY`, `COST-S8-CONTRACT-COSTS-EX2`, `BRK-JS-02-GIFT-CARDS-BREAKAGE-ESCHEAT`) the reader's rows over the round-tripped trace equal the engine's `book.balances` in every `_txn` column and every same-currency `_functional` column, row for row — no derived oracle, no key changed.

**READ-2 (amended, D-98 72) — fourth grain, stage 11.** The engine's member rows also carry `cost_asset_carrying_txn` and `loss_provision_txn` from stage 11 (`s11_costs_loss/balances.py`: each row takes the sum of the T-CON-16 carrying amounts of its member contract's cost assets at the period end and the sum of the T-CON-17 required provisions of its loss units, else 0 — the T-CON-09 default; no trace node is cited). Their producer nodes are `carrying_amount:<contract>/<asset key>:<period>` (asset grain; the asset key is the cost asset's event key `<contract>/EV-<n>`) and `loss_provision_required:<loss unit>:<period>` (unit grain: `<contract>` under POL-150 `CONTRACT`, `<contract>/<obligation>` under `POB`); the reader sums each per (contract head, period) as signed int minor units into the member row `<contract>@<contracting entity>` — the contracting entity from the persisted obligation rows of that contract, else the single entity in which the contract has member rows, else refused by name — cites no node (as the engine does not), fills 0 where no producer node exists (stage 11's own default) and never a `_functional` column (the engine emits none). Verified against the engine for `COST-S8-CONTRACT-COSTS-EX2` (252 carrying nodes, every period), `LOSS-GE-03-LOSS-CONTRACT-PROVISION` and `LOSS-S7-LOSS-OWN` (every row) before the code change; the corpus property test carries these keys.

Native outcomes re-run (`.run/f-rps-e1/native-outcomes-d98-72.log`): POS-CHK-012 `not_run` (82 / 0 refused), POS-CHK-117 `not_run` (75 / 0 refused), EX42 `failed` on ENC-6's stop — unchanged. No key, oracle or expected figure changed; the original 5,000 / 2,800 expectations and lineage of RET-JS-03 are untouched. Q-12 / READ-2 acceptance stays OPEN (Codex's call).

## 25. READ2-R2 (population) — the supervisor's ruling extending D-98 72 (docs first)

Supervisor's words, verbatim: Codex's final READ2 review is in — `PRODUCTION-F-RPS-READ2-REVIEW-712d877.md` (SHA256 `d26ad163…`), manifest `8e67dcb9…` (43 artifacts), exact source 712d8773 / runtime 67aee4c6: original native balance assertions 17/17; same-trace reconstruction 15/17. **R1** is the D-98 72 defect already in the current slice (§24 / §24.1). **Distinct READ2-R2 — output population is wrong (OPEN):** six original group / checkpoint outputs contain 12 native balance rows versus 24 reader rows — 12 surplus GROUP-level row observations and no missing native subject / period pairs; the POS-012 phantom group row repeats the USD 30,000 contract asset, the POS-117 phantom repeats USD 2,000 CA / USD 3,000 UR; the original comparator ignores extra subjects, so it passed. This is observed reconstruction overpopulation (not a claim of external-report double counting). **Ruling, extending D-98 72:** the reader emits one row per actual canonical MEMBER identity of the selected group and period — the members as persisted (the computation's contract / obligation version rows, the engine's own member set), never a group-level subject and never a CG-prefix heuristic (valid contract ids may carry that prefix); add full subject / period population assertions (exact set equality against the native rows) beside the original financial amounts, so overpopulation fails. **Rule READ-2 (amended, READ2-R2 — member identities are the persisted ones).** The member set the reader opens rows for is read from the computation's persisted rows, not from the plan: the `contract` rows whose `combination_group_id` is the group (`external_id` = the canonical member identity) and the group's `combination_group.code`; `output_bundle` compares them with the plan's `member_contract_keys` / `group_key` and refuses by name on any difference (the plan is not the source; a difference is a real discrepancy). `trace_balances` emits exactly one row per (persisted member `<contract>@<entity>`, period) that the member-grain nodes open; a `<X>@<entity>` node whose `X` is the persisted group code and not a member is the group's own row and is never emitted; a node whose `X` is neither a member nor the group refuses by name; no prefix of any id is ever inspected. Population assertion: the corpus property test asserts exact set equality of (subject, period) between the reader's rows and the engine's native rows for every checkpoint bundle, beside the amounts, so overpopulation fails; a dedicated test replays the six original outputs (POS-CHK-012 end-of-p1 and end-of-p2 for both groups, POS-CHK-117 march-close, RET-JS-03 shipment) and asserts 12 rows, none group-level. Fail-first: the reader at 9550d6e3 (before §24.1) replayed on those six outputs from the same hash-verified traces → 24 rows, 12 of them the groups' own subjects (`.run/f-rps-e1/fail-first-read2-r2.log`).

## 26. Codex financial retest at cecd4ed0 — READ2-R1 / R2 closed narrowly; source-only follow-up: one canonical (encoded) attribution key (docs first)

`PRODUCTION-F-RPS-FINANCIAL-RETEST-cecd4ed.md` (SHA-256 `14b5f31103052984b02a137924bd89ca4b731b5756db000f6c2d1451beb3a31b`, verified), manifest `91742ad0f1c140013718618a4fb4a7ba4bf871087f515a1b89b48983e4dd28db` (22 artifacts). Supervisor's words, verbatim: **READ2-R1 / R2 closed narrowly at the measured native / inert interface** — 17/17 original monetary assertions (RET-JS-03 USD 5,000 refund liability / USD 2,800 return asset), 12/12 complete native balance rows with no surplus group rows incl. all amount columns and trace-node attribution; six input bundles / native output hashes / traces identical to baseline; 140 engine files unchanged; member recognition by exact canonical membership. The original 21 controls stay **raw 20/21** — the sole failure is the literal comparison to the saved old overpopulated 24-row output (also lacking the native zero stage-11 columns); the expected old JSON is preserved and a separate native population oracle shows full 12-row equality — never rewritten as 21/21. Harness changes were snapshot / commit / output constants only; the original population needed no new obligation identity rows, so the persisted obligation mapping was not exercised there. **New source-only follow-up:** `workspace_reads.py` stores `contract_entities` keyed by the ENCODED `ob_subject.partition("/")[0]` (example stored `C%2F1`) while the lookup uses `_decode_component(head)` (queried `C/1`); with multiple entity rows the single-row fallback cannot disambiguate even though the persisted obligation mapping was supplied (root checked the exact source; not a measured financial counterexample). Fix: one canonical representation for the lookup key — decode at store time or encode at lookup, stated in the record — and an actual-producer regression at stage 11 with an encoded contract id (a "/" in the id) and multiple entity rows, built as a separately labelled world (never altering the original key financial inputs). Not accepted, as before: non-zero stage 11, foreign-functional balances, command / actor / DB / full keys / full RPS.

**Rule READ-2 (amended — attribution keys are the CV-21 ENCODED components).** Every map the reader uses to attribute a producer node to a member row is keyed by the encoded component exactly as the trace node ids carry it and as `obligation_subject_key` / `contract_entity_subject_key` produce it: the stage-11 `contract_entities` map (obligation subject head → contracting entity) is keyed by the encoded head and looked up with the node's encoded head — nothing is decoded for a lookup; `_decode_component` serves refusal messages only. Chosen form: encoded at both ends (the sources already are; decoding would be the transformation). Regression world (separately labelled, in memory only): the `COST-S8-CONTRACT-COSTS-EX2` key re-validated with its contract relabelled `C-COST/2` (so the encoded component is `C-COST%2F2`) and a second entity `US02` (same currency and calendar) added as the line's performing entity, so the tenant holds two entity rows while the engine — as observed on that world and stated at §26.1 — opens member rows at the contracting entity `US01` only, and stage 11's cost asset must land on that row through the persisted obligation mapping (the two-member-row ambiguity is the separately synthetic control of §26.1, not this producer world; wording corrected at §28 per Codex) — the original key file and its financial inputs are untouched; the property `reader rows == engine rows` runs on that world too. Fail-first: the reader at 9a2809d3 on that world (`.run/f-rps-e1/fail-first-encoded-key.log`).

### 26.1 Applied: the encoded attribution key at 53d5ed10

Fail-first (`.run/f-rps-e1/fail-first-encoded-key.log`, at 88849479): on the relabelled world the `carrying_amount` node's head is `C-COST%2F2` and the persisted mapping's key is `C-COST%2F2`, but the lookup used `_decode_component(head)` = `C-COST/2` → `None`; the single-member-row fallback answered `US01` instead, so the persisted mapping was never used, and with a contract whose member rows spanned two entities the fallback would have refused. Codex's source-only finding reproduced on an actual producer trace.

Chosen representation — **encoded at both ends**: the trace node heads and the persisted obligation subject keys (`obligation_subject_key`) are CV-21 encoded already, so the reader looks the stage-11 map up by the node's encoded head and never decodes for a lookup (`_decode_component` serves refusal messages only). Semantic tightened with it: when the persisted mapping (`obligation_entities`) is supplied it is authoritative — a contract it does not name refuses by name ("the persisted obligation rows name no obligation of that contract"); the single-member-row fallback serves only a call made without the mapping (the direct-trace unit path), and a contract with member rows in several entities and no mapping refuses ("no single member row and no persisted obligation mapping was supplied") instead of guessing. `_book` always supplies the mapping from the `obligation_version` rows, so the database platform never reaches the fallback.

Tests (`test_platform_trace_balances.py`, now 14): `test_stage_11_attribution_uses_the_encoded_persisted_mapping_on_a_producer_world` — the COST-S8-CONTRACT-COSTS-EX2 key re-validated in memory with contract `C-COST/2` and entity `US02` as the line's performing entity (`_relabelled_cost_world`; the key file and its inputs untouched), computed by the engine: the reader over the round-tripped trace equals the engine's `cost_asset_carrying_txn` on `C-COST%2F2@US01` through the mapping `{C-COST%2F2/L1-OUTSOURCE: US01}` (non-zero), an empty supplied mapping refuses by name, no mapping falls back to the single row; `test_encoded_head_lookup_disambiguates_two_member_rows_where_the_fallback_cannot` — a hand-built trace with `C%2F1@US01` and `C%2F1@US02` member rows and one `carrying_amount:C%2F1/EV-000001` node: the mapping places 12345 on US01 and 0 on US02; without a mapping the reader refuses. Observed on the producer world (not asserted as an oracle): the engine emits member rows only at the contracting entity, so the performing entity `US02` opens no row — the two-entity ambiguity is exercised by the hand-built case. The relabelled world's `cost_asset_carrying_txn` at FY2026-P01 (14749999) differs from the original key's (14821428): Codex isolated this to the ENGINE (ENG-COST-ENC-1, §28 — `s11_costs_loss/capitalise.py` filters the estimate ledger with the raw contract-key prefix while the pins are encoded, so the renewal is missed on an encoded id), routed to the stage-11 owner (D-98 76); not a reader regression, not a different world's amount, and the key and its expected amount are not revised. `unit/answer_keys` **236 passed** (was 234; count read from `.run/f-rps-e1/suite-enc.log`); `make lint` OK; `make typecheck` OK. No key, oracle or expected figure changed; no plan or conversion change (native outcomes stand). Not accepted (Codex's boundaries): non-zero stage 11 on the original keys, foreign-functional balances, command / actor / DB / full keys / full RPS. Holding for merge prep after this report.

## 27. Codex persisted-identity review at 0638c590 / 9a2809d3 — F-RPS-ID-R1, historical membership (D-98 74; docs first)

`PRODUCTION-F-RPS-PERSISTED-IDENTITY-REVIEW-0638c59.md` (SHA-256 `d27b6c732f25a1aad71bf9a81cce68e82b68f0260fc534131a0410ca2c17c911`, verified), manifest `2684465a42669d8e4a2da0d4e4da6c5d0e5a366395ae9f65ba0c2b97f985033a` (32 artifacts). Supervisor's words, verbatim: 17/17 monetary and 12/12 complete native rows remain correct; the initial missing `combination_group` fixture refusals preserved (the disclosed adapter adds only the original bundle's group id / code); original controls raw 20/21 unchanged; eight ordinary identity worlds 24/24 incl. extra / omitted members and exclusion of an unrelated group; the query reads the whole group, not a plan-selected subset. **New F-RPS-ID-R1 — reproduced historical identity defect (OPEN):** the reader selects a historical version by `known_at`, then derives membership from the CURRENT `contract.combination_group_id`; in the separately labelled inert case a member moves to a new group one second AFTER the original cutoff — native T-CON-04 history still assigns it to the old group at that cutoff, and the domain's `queries._members` already constructs the half-open historical query and groups the supplied result correctly — but the reader never consults that history, sees current members = [], and refuses the valid earlier checkpoint (no DB or actual move command executed). **Ruling D-98 74 (applies READ-1's as-of principle to identity):** membership for a checkpoint is the complete persisted population at the requested `known_at` — rows with `valid_from_known_at <= cutoff AND (valid_to_known_at > cutoff OR valid_to_known_at IS NULL)` — read through the same T-CON-04 history semantics `queries._members` uses (reuse it or mirror it exactly), then compared with the planned set; true extra / missing members AT THAT CUTOFF still refuse by name; never the current group column for a historical checkpoint; preserve all original amounts, population and the current-mismatch controls. Regression: the original inputs unchanged + Codex's separately labelled move-after-cutoff world (member moved one second after the cutoff → the earlier checkpoint reconstructs; a checkpoint after the move sees the new membership). Codex's supplemental raw 27/30 retained as measured (two failures = this defect; one = a harness SQL expectation missing the `::UUID` cast, adjudicated as a harness assumption); the encoded stage-11 map fix at 53d5ed10 stays separate and source-identical.

**Rule READ-2 (amended, D-98 74 — membership is read as of the cutoff).** `output_bundle` reads the group's members at the checkpoint's `known_at` from the T-CON-04 `combination_group_member` rows of the group — exactly `queries._members`' predicate, mirrored over the row source: `valid_from_known_at <= known_at AND (valid_to_known_at IS NULL OR valid_to_known_at > known_at)` — and maps their `contract_id`s to external ids through the `contract` rows; the current `contract.combination_group_id` is never consulted for a historical checkpoint. The planned `member_contract_keys` are compared with that population; a member the plan names that the history does not hold at the cutoff, or one the history holds that the plan does not name, refuses by name naming the cutoff. Codex's inert move-after-cutoff world is the regression: the original group's row `valid_from = known_at − 1 day, valid_to = known_at + 1 s`, the new group's row `valid_from = known_at + 1 s, valid_to NULL`, the current contract row pointing at the new group — the checkpoint at `known_at` reconstructs with the original membership; a checkpoint one minute after the move sees the new membership (the old group empty at that cutoff → the planned member refuses by name; the new group holds it). The current-mismatch controls (other members, another group code) stand. Fail-first: the reader at 0937e17a on that world (`.run/f-rps-e1/fail-first-id-r1.log`).

### 27.1 Applied: membership as of the cutoff at f0119908

Fail-first (`.run/f-rps-e1/fail-first-id-r1.log`, at 87c2c850): Codex's inert move-after-cutoff world over the reads fixture — the old group's T-CON-04 row `valid_from = cutoff − 1 day, valid_to = cutoff + 1 s`, the new group's row from `cutoff + 1 s`, the current `contract.combination_group_id` moved to the new group — `output_bundle` at the original cutoff **refused** "the plan names members ['C-1'] but the persisted group CG-C-1 holds []": the reader read the CURRENT group column for a historical checkpoint. Codex's defect reproduced.

Applied (`workspace_reads.py`): `PersistedReads.members_at(group_id, known_at)` mirrors `queries._members`' predicate over the `combination_group_member` rows of the group — `valid_from_known_at <= known_at AND (valid_to_known_at IS NULL OR valid_to_known_at > known_at)` — and maps the member `contract_id`s to external ids through the `contract` rows (a member contract the table does not hold refuses by name); `output_bundle` compares that population with the plan's members and refuses by name naming the cutoff on any difference ("… holds […] at <known_at> (rule READ-2, D-98 74)"); the current `contract.combination_group_id` is no longer read anywhere in the reader. Mirrored rather than reused: `_members` takes a SQLAlchemy session and the reader works over the row source; the predicate is the same expression, stated beside it.

Tests: `test_membership_is_read_as_of_the_cutoff_from_the_t_con_04_history` — `members_at` answers `("C-1",)` for the old group and `()` for the new at the cutoff, and the reverse one minute after the move; the checkpoint at the cutoff reconstructs (the member's rows and `status_in_book`); the checkpoint after the move refuses by name for the old group ("holds [] at …"); a plan naming another member refuses; the fixture's contract carries a T-CON-04 row valid since before the computation so every other reads test keeps its population. `unit/answer_keys` **237 passed** (was 236; count read from `.run/f-rps-e1/suite-id.log`); `make lint` OK; `make typecheck` OK. No key, oracle or expected figure changed; no plan or conversion change (native outcomes stand); the encoded stage-11 map fix at 53d5ed10 is source-identical. Codex's supplemental 27/30 stays as measured. Holding for merge prep after this report.

## 28. Codex's final stage-11 report at 0937e17a / 53d5ed10 — ENG-COST-ENC-1 (engine) and F-RPS-ST11-R1 (reader; D-98 76 routing) — docs first

`PRODUCTION-F-RPS-STAGE11-REVIEW-0937e17.md` (SHA-256 `0f652f8398ca3f714f99e0fb990416739d62bbb0fd4395fde5cbec0ce6c9f3ef`, verified), manifest `d44b66b24beb0b08f14a81f53c8037b5d5581e1acdcef4c3b8af4951bb3f4b6e` (20 artifacts). Supervisor's words, verbatim: two separated findings. (a) **ENG-COST-ENC-1 — an ENGINE canonical-identity bug, not the lane's:** relabelling only the contract id to `C-COST/2` moves COST-S8's P01 carrying from USD 148,214.28 to 147,499.99 because `s11_costs_loss/capitalise.py:250–259` filters the estimate ledger with the raw contract-key prefix while the pins are encoded (the renewal is missed; the 60-month term kept) — routed to the stage-11 owner (D-98 76); the key and the expected amount are not revised; the §26.1 observation (14749999 vs 14821428) is explained by that bug, not by a different world. (b) **F-RPS-ST11-R1 confirmed:** correct encoded attribution reconstructs all 84 native rows, but changing only the supplied mapping to `US02` (an existing input entity with no member balance row) silently drops the native USD 147,499.99 and zero-fills `US01` with no error — `workspace_reads.py` skips the absent mapped row and zero-fills. Fix: when a supplied attribution names an entity for which the contract opens no member row at that period, refuse by the named node / contract / period (never drop the amount, never zero-fill the original row); preserve the valid authoritative mapping, the empty / wrong-head refusal and the documented None / single-entity fallback; fail-first with Codex's US02 world. Also align §26's wording: the actual producer world populates US01 only (one member-entity row); the two-member-row case is the separately synthetic control — §26.1 already says so; §26 must not overstate it (corrected above). Raw main 20/23 stands as measured (two amount failures = the engine bug; one = the reader refusal failure); the prior 17 amounts / 12 rows are source-bound, not rerun; the historical-membership fix f0119908 / ae09fa8b is under separate retest.

**Rule READ-2 (amended, F-RPS-ST11-R1 — a mapped entity must open the row).** When the supplied persisted mapping places a stage-11 sum on `<contract>@<entity>` and the member grain opened no row for that subject at that period, the reader refuses by name — the column, the contract, the period and the mapped entity — and never drops the amount nor zero-fills the contract's other row; the engine's own `continue` for a missing row is mirrored only for the stage-10 summed measures whose row set the member grain defines. The authoritative mapping (valid entity), the empty / wrong-head refusals and the None / single-member-row fallback stand as documented in §26.1. Fail-first: Codex's US02 world — the relabelled producer trace unchanged, the supplied mapping's value `US02` — through the reader at ae09fa8b (`.run/f-rps-e1/fail-first-st11-r1.log`).

### 28.1 Applied: F-RPS-ST11-R1 at cbbd89be

Fail-first (`.run/f-rps-e1/fail-first-st11-r1.log`, at ae09fa8b): Codex's US02 world — the relabelled producer trace unchanged (84 native rows; FY2026-P01 `C-COST%2F2@US01` `cost_asset_carrying_txn` 14749999), the supplied mapping's value changed to `US02` (an input entity with no member balance row) — the reader returned 84 rows with **no error** and `US01`'s `cost_asset_carrying_txn` **0**: the amount was dropped by the missing-row skip and the row zero-filled. Codex's defect reproduced.

Applied (`workspace_reads.trace_balances`, stage-11 attribution): when the entity came from the supplied mapping and `grouped` holds no `(<contract>@<entity>, period)` row, the reader refuses by name — "stage-11 <column> of '<contract>' <period> is mapped to entity '<entity>', for which the contract opens no member row at that period (rule READ-2, F-RPS-ST11-R1)"; the amount is never dropped and the contract's other row is never zero-filled. The engine's own `continue` for a missing row is mirrored only for the stage-10 summed measures, whose row set the member grain defines. Preserved as documented: the valid authoritative mapping (`US01` → equals the engine on the producer world), the empty-mapping refusal ("name no obligation of that contract"), the None / single-member-row fallback and its two-row ambiguity refusal, the two-member-row synthetic control. Tests: the `US02` refusal on the producer world (`test_stage_11_attribution_uses_the_encoded_persisted_mapping_on_a_producer_world`) and a `US03` refusal on the synthetic two-row case (`test_encoded_head_lookup_disambiguates_two_member_rows_where_the_fallback_cannot`). `unit/answer_keys` **237 passed** (unchanged count — the assertions were added inside the two existing tests; read from `.run/f-rps-e1/suite-st11.log`); `make lint` OK; `make typecheck` OK. No key, oracle or expected figure changed; ENG-COST-ENC-1 is recorded and not acted on (the stage-11 owner's, D-98 76); the encoded-key fix (53d5ed10) and the as-of membership fix (f0119908) are source-identical. Codex's raw main 20/23 stands as measured. Holding for merge prep after this report.

## 31. PLAT-ACT-1 + PLAT-OVR-1 — two DB-test defects from the batch ci on main 065e7f65: the stand-in principal map (`db_personas`) and the override's approval linkage; questions Q-13 and Q-14 returned; ENG-C8's 89933d40 reviewed

Dispatch (team-lead, 2026-09-20 ~02:40 PDT): the batch's ci stage on `065e7f65` (`run-065e7f65/ci.log`) fails `backend/tests/domain/answer_keys/test_platform_ledger_resolver_db.py::test_ledger_resolver_ids_equal_rows` with `NotProvisioned: not run — databases not provisioned: WORLD tenant currencies: no principal mapped for actor 'ak-approver'; Workspace.uow(None) is never used (rule ACT-1)` at `workspace_adapter.py:976`. Find which side is wrong — the plan step's actor or the world's persona provisioning — and fix it there without loosening ACT-1; fail-first CPU test in the runner's plan / principal-map shape; the DB test stays NOT RUN here; lint guard; captured statuses (typecheck, lint, focused runner suite, CPU set, 220 selection); report the head. (The §31 note on ENG-C4's landing still follows separately; at `065e7f65` ENG-C4 has not landed — `test_platform_runner.py` still carries the §30 diagnostic.)

Merge (`.run/f-rps-e1/merge-065e7f65.log`): `git merge 065e7f65` from `8652a10e` — fast-forward, rc=0, no conflicts.

**Diagnosis (evidence, all CPU reads of the tree at 065e7f65).**

1. *Direct cause — the DB test's persona provisioning, not ACT-1 and not the plan.* `test_ledger_resolver_ids_equal_rows` (written in §17, before ACT-1 in §22; never run — no database) calls `WorkspaceAdapter.for_database(loaded, place)` with **no principal map** and skips the PROVISION / PERSONAS steps (maya and marcus stand in). `_principal_for` therefore has an empty map and refuses the first persona-attributed step by name — exactly the ci text; `for_database`'s own comment says every actor needs a proven principal. The refusal is ACT-1 behaving correctly; `Workspace.principal` (the `maya_principal` default) is precisely what ACT-1 forbids using implicitly. The siblings `test_platform_following_db.py` and `test_platform_reads_db.py` do not go through the adapter's invoker (they drive `place.uow()` and the reads directly) and are not affected.

2. *Q-13 (returned) — the `approver` role code does not exist.* ACT-1's `ROLE_CODES["ak-approver"] = ("controller", "approver")` mirrors DG-AK-41 ("`ak-approver` (controller and approver roles)"), the PERSONAS invite step (`roles: "controller,approver"`) and the unit fakes. The platform's role catalogue (04 §14.3 T-PLT-09; `erev_api/auth/permissions.py` `DEFAULT_ROLES`; seeded by `domain/platform/provisioning.py`) has ten system roles — `revenue_accountant, revenue_reviewer, controller, ssp_analyst, ssp_approver, integration_admin, tenant_admin, auditor, viewer, service_account` — and no `approver`; `support.rows.insert_role_assignment` selects the tenant's `role` row by code (`scalar_one`), so `assign(marcus, "approver")` cannot succeed, and a full database run's PERSONAS invite would refuse at `role_id("approver")` (READ-7). The DB stand-in therefore cannot honestly hold `approver`; claiming it on an in-memory principal would fabricate a role the database does not hold. Options for the supervisor (docs first, not decided here): (a) DG-AK-41 names `controller` only (ROLE_CODES, the invite step and the fakes follow); (b) the PERSONAS phase creates a tenant `approver` role through `domain/platform/roles.create_role` (a plan step, role content and its approval); (c) the platform seeds an `approver` system role (a T-PLT-09 change). The lane's DB worlds give marcus `controller` + `tenant_admin` today.

3. *Q-14 (returned) — who applies tenant settings.* `PUT /tenant-currencies` needs `settings.manage` (`api/v1/currencies.py` module docstring), which only `tenant_admin` holds (`DEFAULT_ROLES`: `tenant_admin` True, every other role False). The plan attributes the WORLD step "tenant currencies" to `ak-approver` (controller + approver), who could not perform it through the API; the domain handler `put_tenant_currencies` carries no permission check (the API layer does), so the adapter's direct domain call would succeed for the wrong persona. DG-AK-41 says the runner "applies `world` through commands … approving as `ak-approver`" and is silent on who applies tenant settings. Candidates: the OPERATOR (the provisioning operator; note `ROLE_CODES` has no `operator` entry, so ACT-1 checks no roles for it — a gap to close with the ruling) or a persona holding `tenant_admin`. Not changed here: the plan mirrors the dev-guide.

Answer to the dispatch's question: for the ci failure the provisioning side is wrong (fixed below); the plan's actor for "tenant currencies" is also wrong at the API layer (Q-14) but is a docs-first decision.

**Applied (test side only; ACT-1, the plan, the engine and the keys untouched).**

- `backend/tests/support/answer_keys/db_personas.py` (new): the stand-in personas' shape — `PERSONA_ROLES` (`ak-preparer`: `revenue_accountant`; `ak-approver`: `controller`, `tenant_admin` — T-PLT-09 codes only), `LEDGER_RESOLVER_HANDLERS` (the five world handlers the DB test runs), `MemberLike` (Protocol: `user_id`, `tenant_id`, `membership_id`), `persona_principal(persona, someone)` (a `Principal` with the member's ids, the persona's roles sorted and their `DEFAULT_ROLES` permissions over every entity; unknown persona or non-T-PLT-09 code refused by name), `principal_map(preparer, approver)`, `actors_of(steps)`.

## 32. PLAT-ACT-1b — D-98 candidate 107 applied: Q-13 (the approver holds the controller role only) and Q-14 (tenant settings are the operator's); docs first (dev-guide 1.38)

Rulings (team-lead, 2026-09-20 ~05:15 PDT; D-98 candidate 107, recorded): **Q-13 → option (a)** — there is no `approver` platform role and none is created; approvals are done by the roles the routing rules name; `ROLE_CODES["ak-approver"] = ("controller",)`, the PERSONAS invite becomes `controller`, the fakes follow, DG-AK-41 is amended docs first ("controller and approver roles" → the controller role) with dev-guide revision row **1.38** (sheet: P5 1.36, F-CLO 1.37, F-RPS 1.38); the pinned `lacks roles ['approver']` diagnostic is removed once the rule is applied (it becomes the fail-first evidence here). **Q-14 → the OPERATOR** — tenant settings are operator work: the plan attributes "tenant currencies" (and any other step whose API needs `settings.manage`) to the OPERATOR persona; `ROLE_CODES` gains an `operator` entry carrying the roles provisioning grants the operator (incl. `tenant_admin`) so ACT-1 checks it like every other actor; the plan change is docs first where DG-AK-41 states the step's actor. **ACT-2** follows as the next slice (dispatch to follow): the adapter's invoker checks the step's declared API permission against the persona's principal before any direct domain call.

**Docs first (`docs/dev-guide.md`).** DG-AK-41 now reads: `ak-approver` "(the controller role; there is no `approver` platform role — approvals are done by the roles the routing rules name; rev 1.38, D-98 candidate 107)"; the runner "applies `world` through commands as `ak-preparer`, except tenant settings — steps whose API needs `settings.manage`, such as `PUT /tenant-currencies` — which the provisioning operator applies (the operator holds the provisioned `tenant_admin` grant; rev 1.38, D-98 candidate 107)". Revision row 1.38 (2026-09-20) inserted after the tree's latest row 1.28 (rows 1.29–1.37 are other lanes' rows landing on main) and the header's first entry is 1.38; `test_governed_docs_revisions.py` + `test_repository_layout.py`: 10 passed.

**Which WORLD steps move (permission reading of the API layer, `erev_api/api/v1/*.py` module docstrings against `DEFAULT_ROLES`).** `PUT /tenant-currencies` needs `settings.manage` exclusively — `tenant_admin` only → OPERATOR. Calendars and `generate-year` (`create_calendar`, `ensure_fiscal_year`) and entities / books (`create_entity`, `put_entity_book`) need any of `masterdata.maintain` / `settings.manage` — `revenue_accountant` holds `masterdata.maintain` → PREPARER stays valid. `open` (`open_period`) needs `period.close` (or `settings.manage` while setup is incomplete) — `controller` and `revenue_accountant` hold `period.close` → PREPARER stays valid. Customers and products need `contract.read`; GL accounts `config.author` (`revenue_accountant` holds it). So exactly one WORLD step moves: "tenant currencies" → OPERATOR. (The PERSONAS invite / role assignment were already the operator's.)

**Applied.**

## 34. ACT-2 — the invoker checks each step's declared API permission before any direct domain call (D-98 candidate 107, second half; docs first under dev-guide 1.38)

Dispatch (team-lead, 2026-09-20 ~06:05 PDT): the `WorkspaceAdapter`'s invoker checks, before ANY direct domain call, the step's declared API permission against the acting persona's principal — the permissions its roles grant, resolved the way the API layer resolves them (reuse the API's constants / resolver, no parallel table of truths) — and refuses by name (`ACT-2: actor 'ak-preparer' lacks permission settings.manage for step 'tenant currencies'`); the plan (or a step registry) declares each step's API permission explicitly — one table step → permission, sourced from the route decorators / dependency the step corresponds to — with a CPU test that every WORLD / PERSONAS / SCENARIO step has a declared permission equal to the API's; fail-first: "tenant currencies" as a persona lacking the permission → refused (today's adapter would pass), as the OPERATOR → permitted, a declaration the API does not require → refused (the table must not overstate); the ledger-resolver DB test unchanged apart from the map (NOT RUN); docs first under the same dev-guide row 1.38 (not landed → amended in place).

**Docs first (`docs/dev-guide.md`, row 1.38 amended in place).** DG-AK-41 gains: "Before any direct domain call, the adapter's invoker checks the step's declared API permission — the permission the step's route requires, or the permission set its handler authorises for a per-subject route, declared once per step in the runner's step registry and equal to the API's — against the acting principal's permissions, resolved as the API layer resolves them, and refuses by name (`ACT-2: actor '<persona>' lacks permission <code> for step '<step>'`), so a persona that could not perform the step through the API cannot perform it through the adapter (rev 1.38, D-98 candidate 107, ACT-2)"; the 1.38 log row carries the ACT-2 clause. `test_governed_docs_revisions.py` + `test_repository_layout.py`: 10 passed.

**Where the API's truth lives (CPU reads).** (1) A guarded route declares `Depends(command(<code>))` / `Depends(require(<code>))`; `erev_api.auth.dependencies.require` passes when `code in principal.permissions` and hangs the code on the dependency, and `api.deps.GuardedRoute` records it as `openapi_extra["x-erev-permission"]` (API-C-03) — readable from the application without a database (`create_app(app_settings)`; the routers are `_IncludedRouter`s whose `original_router.routes` hold the `APIRoute`s: 351 routes, 284 with a recorded permission, 29 per-subject). (2) A per-subject route (`command(per_subject=True)`) records no permission; its handler authorises through `erev_api.domain.platform.attachments.authorize(ctx, codes, …)`, which passes when `codes & principal.permissions` is non-empty — the sets are module constants: `reference.commands.MAINTAIN_PERMISSIONS` = {masterdata.maintain, settings.manage} (calendars, generate-year, entities, entity books), `reference.commands.FX_PERMISSIONS` = {config.author, masterdata.maintain} (FX sets / versions / submit), `reference.periods.open_permissions(setup_completed)` = {period.close} ∪ {settings.manage while `tenant.setup_completed_at IS NULL`} (period open). (3) Approvals (`approvals/{id}/approve`, per-subject) authorise by the subject spec: `erev_api.approvals.subjects.spec_for(subject_type).required_permission` (POLICY_OVERRIDE / CONTRACT_ACTIVATION / COMBINATION_GROUP → contract.approve; ESTIMATE_VERSION → estimate.approve; REGISTRY / RULE_SET / POB_TEMPLATE / ACCOUNT_MAPPING / FX_RATE_SET versions → config.approve; JUDGEMENT_RECORD → judgement.review; SSP_BOOK_VERSION → ssp.approve). Activation and the compute job run as the SYSTEM principal (no route); provisioning is `POST /api/v1/operator/tenants` (the operator's platform route, no tenant permission).

**Applied.**
- `backend/tests/support/answer_keys/step_permissions.py` (new): `StepPermission(kind, method, path, required, subject)` with kinds `route` (one code, the route's recorded permission), `handler` (per-subject route; the domain constant object itself), `approval` (resolved at check time from the subject — `decide` from the latest committed submission via `LedgerResolver.pending_subject_type()` (RES-2 / READ-3 `SUBJECTS`), `ssp_approve` from its fixed SSP_BOOK_VERSION subject), `system`, `operator`; `PERMISSIONS` — 63 entries covering every `erev_api.` handler of `H` plus `PRESET_HANDLER` (approval 2, handler 8, operator 1, route 50, system 2); `required_permissions(call, ledger)`; `check(call, principal, ledger)` — a `system` step requires the SYSTEM principal; otherwise refuses by name unless `required & principal.permissions` (single code: `lacks permission <code>`; per-subject: `lacks permission any of <a>, <b>`); an undeclared handler is refused by name, never waved through. Assumption stated in the module: world steps open periods during tenant setup (`open_permissions(setup_completed=False)`); `report_run` (the job body `framework.run_report`) is declared as the `POST /report-runs` route the adapter's run performs; the CHECKPOINT reads are declared with their GET routes although the adapter reads them through SQL.
- `workspace_adapter.real_invoker`: after ACT-1's `_principal_for` and before `request_models.adapt` and the unit of work, `step_permissions.check(call, principal, ledger)`; `WorkspaceJobs.journal_run` / `report_run` check `journal.run` / `report.run` for the job persona before their unit of work (`_permitted`).
- `ledger_resolver.LedgerResolver.pending_subject_type()`.
- Fakes: the unit `_principal(...)` in `test_platform_workspace_adapter.py` and `test_platform_ledger_resolver.py` now carry the permissions `DEFAULT_ROLES` grants their roles (as the API resolves them), with an explicit override for the ACT-2 refusal cases; `db_personas.persona_principal` already did.
- Tests: `test_platform_step_permissions.py` (new; 4): every plan step with an API handler is declared and the registry equals the handler table; every declared route exists in the application and its recorded `x-erev-permission` equals the declaration (or is absent for a per-subject route, whose set is the domain constant), and every declared code is a permission the API knows (`spec(code)`); a decision requires the pending subject's approval permission for all ten submitting handlers and is refused with no submission; system / operator steps declare no tenant permission. `test_platform_workspace_adapter.py::test_act_2_the_steps_api_permission_is_checked_before_any_domain_call`: a principal holding the `tenant_admin` role but not `settings.manage` is refused `ACT-2: actor 'operator' lacks permission settings.manage for step 'tenant currencies'` before any unit of work; a preparer without `masterdata.maintain` is refused `lacks permission any of masterdata.maintain, settings.manage for step 'entity …'`; the operator's provisioned grant reaches the unit of work.

Fail-first (`.run/f-rps-e1/fail-first-act-2.log`): tests written first — the registry module absent (collection `ModuleNotFoundError`, rc=2), then the adapter module alone against today's invoker: 1 failed / 7 passed, rc=1 — the ACT-2 case reached the unit of work unrefused. After (`.run/f-rps-e1/act-2-after-fix.log`): the three modules 24 passed, rc=0.

Scope note: ACT-2 checks permission membership, as `require` and `authorize` do; the API's MFA step-up for `requires_mfa` permissions and the entity-scope narrowing of `require_for_entity` are session and object concerns the adapter does not model — stated, not claimed.

**Ruling (supervisor, D-98 candidate 107a, extends 107).** Answer-key personas map to the platform roles that ACTUALLY carry each operation's grant in the provisioned permission catalogue; the plan's operation → persona composition is test-side (never an original input or expected value) and follows the governed operation-owner statements; author / approver separation is preserved per operation family (the persona that creates / submits an SSP book version never approves it — `ak-ssp-analyst` = `ssp_analyst`, `ak-ssp-approver` = `ssp_approver`); journal runs are executed by a persona holding `journal.run` (`revenue_accountant` / `ak-preparer`) with `report.run` remaining the controller's; personas NEVER receive fabricated permissions and no check is weakened; a governed operation-owner statement that assigns an operation to a role the catalogue does not grant is reported by row, not fixed by granting; sweep every original planned operation of the five plans against the catalogue. Exactly Codex's (a), (b), (c); nothing else in the five plans is misattributed (config lifecycles: `config.author` → `revenue_accountant`, `config.approve` → `controller`; contracts / estimates / events → `revenue_accountant`; approvals of CONTRACT_ACTIVATION / POLICY_OVERRIDE / ESTIMATE_VERSION / configuration subjects → `controller`; tenant settings, invites, role grants → the operator's `tenant_admin`; report runs → `controller`).

**Governed operation-owner check.** PRD §5.6 (rows 1806–1819) agrees with the catalogue: `ssp.create` → SA only, `ssp.approve` → SP only, `journal.run` → RA only, `report.run` → RA / RR / CO / SA / SP / IA / AU / VW, `config.approve` → CO, `contract.approve` → RR / CO. No governed statement assigns SSP authoring or approval or journal runs to a role the catalogue does not grant — the inconsistency was the plan's test-side composition alone; nothing to route to the PRD / G12 owner. ## 37. PLAT-G4-R1 / R2 — the committed provisioning is recorded before the server clock needs the tenant; the live principal mapping survives `for_database` (Codex packet 1413 items 1–2)

Codex packet production-20260920-1413 items 1–2 (`PRODUCTION-F-RPS-G4-SOURCE-REVIEW-7f5bc463.md`, SHA-256 `e2f0be0e0293385e05551aa291fbf54930b72b10e8287cb8102d90ec01e1b7f4`; manifest `3640e229cc56c12e5b84869a6cf62e386239b932839ad44312abc46273f9a1a0`; 22 artifacts; the five original key hashes preserved; root c2af3a / 21101e), relayed by the lead 2026-09-20 ~07:20 PDT, source-confirmed on `7f5bc463`:

- **R1 — post-provision bootstrap clock.** `database_adapter` binds `workspace.ledger` to the initially EMPTY adapter ledger. After native provisioning returns (its transaction committed), `WorkspaceAdapter._record` called `self.clock` BEFORE appending the provisioning result (`workspace_adapter.py` ~609–618); that clock → `_server_clock` → `RunnerWorkspace.scalar` → `_context` → `tenant_id`, which reads only the still-empty ledger → `NotProvisioned`. The committed transaction was never recorded; `run_platform` labelled the step not run and later tenant / operator lookups could not resolve — a post-commit bookkeeping failure, not proof the database was unchanged or rolled back.
- **R2 — live principal mapping lost at the constructor.** `for_database` constructed `WorkspaceJobs(workspace, principals or {})` (~253); the real `LedgerPrincipals` is initially empty (`__len__` derives from the empty ledger), so `or {}` handed the jobs a NEW empty dict that no later rebinding of `principals.ledger` ever filled — journal / report job lookups would never see a principal, for every persona variant including 107a's split journal / report actors.

**Applied (`workspace_adapter.py`).**
- R1 — `_record` appends the `LedgerEntry` (with the committed result, `server_at` and `known_at` still `None`) and advances the stream heads first, then reads the server clock once and replaces the entry with the stamped one (`dataclasses.replace`). The provisioning result therefore gives the runner workspace its tenant provenance before the clock read that needs it; the stamp is the real post-commit `SELECT clock_timestamp()` read through the tenant context the committed result names — no default principal, no fabricated stamp, no premature success, no fallback. If the clock read refuses, the entry stays (the command did commit) without a stamp and the refusal propagates to the runner, which marks the step not run with the reason — the committed result is never dropped again. `clock_timestamp()` still returns the one post-commit read (`_last_server_at`).
- R2 — `for_database` hands `WorkspaceJobs` the supplied mapping whenever it is not `None` (`{} if principals is None else principals`) — an empty live mapping is never Boolean-tested away.

**Tests (`test_database_platform.py`; fail-first).** `_TenantBoundWorkspace` is the real `RunnerWorkspace` with its two database reads faked to keep their real dependency (they need `_context()` → the ledger's tenant before they can answer). `test_the_committed_provisioning_is_recorded_before_the_server_stamp_needs_the_tenant`: through `for_database` with the initially empty ledger bound as `database_adapter` binds it and a native-shaped committed provisioning result (the native handler replaced by a double returning it — no database): exactly one recorded provision, its `server_at` the one post-commit stamp (`stamps == 1`), `clock_timestamp()` equal to it, then `workspace.tenant_id == TENANT` and `principals["operator"]` resolving to the admin membership. `test_for_database_keeps_the_live_principal_mapping_for_job_runs`: through `for_database` with the real `LedgerPrincipals` over an empty ledger: `adapter.jobs.principals is principals`; before any invite the journal persona is refused by name; after the shared ledger records the provisioning and the two invites, the journal persona resolves to `ak-preparer`'s membership and the report persona to `ak-approver`'s (deferred resolution across the `for_database` boundary; the direct `WorkspaceJobs` tests are kept).

## 42. PLAT-G4-1b (POS-117 `balance_aging`) — the exact remaining prerequisites, the authorised source work done, and how registration and the ORIGINAL four cells bind to the integrated candidate (Codex packet 1756 item 1; record-first)

Lead's dispatch (2026-09-20 ~10:35 PDT, after `14d9107b` landed at `d3a89805`): Codex qualifies the MERGED stamp as the landed runner / dependency record only — at main `platform_plan.py:95–98` retains the RPS-12 / CTR-14 gap, `framework.py:211–248` omits the `balance_aging` builder, `test_platform_plan.py:220–222` pins its absence; §35 says no 1b code change; four POS-117 report cells remain unexecuted. Bounded record-first action: identify the EXACT remaining prerequisite — (a) what Q-2 asks and which rows / cells depend on it, (b) what CTR-14 must persist, (c) which admitted builder DB tests must exist and why the lane cannot run them — then complete any already-authorised source work the prerequisites allow (only if no ruling is needed) and describe how the actual registration and the ORIGINAL POS-117 report assertions bind to the integrated candidate. Never substitute the selected 220, a placeholder or a narrowed oracle for the four cells. No re-merge; no DB provisioning.

**(a) Ruling Q-2 — what it asked, what it decided, what still hangs on it.** Q-2 (§9, asked 2026-09-19) raised five points on RPT-32 / RPT-36 whose catalogue sources (T-CON-15 `contract_cost_asset`, T-CON-16 `cost_asset_version`, T-CON-18 `fx_layer_movement`) are unpersisted: (a) whether the T-SL-04 subledger is acceptable as the interim source; (b) registration in `framework.BUILDERS` ("registering makes the catalogue reports runnable with a source that differs from their definition text"); (c) RPT-32 section 2 by contract + category; (d) JET-09e acceleration under `amortization`; (e) RPT-36 `TOTAL:<role>:<ISO>` rows. The supervisor's ruling (§9 table): the subledger is **accepted as the interim source** until CTR-14 persists T-CON-15/16/18, labelled interim in the builders' docstrings and the 04 rows; **no registration in `framework.BUILDERS` until the builders' DB tests have passed in an admitted run** (registration is user-visible); (c)–(e) accepted as interim presentations. So Q-2 is *ruled*, not pending: no new ruling is needed; what remains is to satisfy its condition — an admitted database run of the builder's DB tests. What depends on it: POS-CHK-117's `march-close` report block (after_seq 7, `period_key FY2026-P03`, `entity_codes [US01]`, `book ASC606`) — exactly four cells: `contract:C-POS-117:UNBILLED_RECEIVABLE` `bucket_0_30` 3000.00 and `bucket_31_90` 0.00; `contract:C-POS-117:CONTRACT_ASSET` `bucket_0_30` 2000.00 and `bucket_31_90` 0.00 — plus, on the test side, the plan gap `REPORT_GAPS["balance_aging"]` (`platform_plan.py`) that turns the step into `not_run` and the pin `test_gaps_per_key` (`'balance_aging' not in framework.BUILDERS`). Nothing else in the five plans depends on the builder (RPT-32 `contract_cost_rollforward` is registered on main since ENG-C8; EX21's four report kinds and EX42's `rpo` are registered).

**(b) CTR-14 — what it must persist, and that it is NOT a prerequisite for the four cells under Q-2.** BUILD_SPEC CTR-14 (`- [ ]`, unbuilt; prerequisite CTR-13) persists T-CON-14 `material_right`, T-CON-15 `contract_cost_asset`, T-CON-16 `cost_asset_version`, T-CON-17 `loss_provision_version`, T-CON-18 `fx_layer_movement` (enums E-83 to E-86, E-92) through migration `NNNN_ctr_14_costs_material_rights_loss_fx` and `domain/contracts/computation.py` (stage 11 / 12 outputs and billing-plan schedule). `balance_aging`'s catalogue definition sources `fx_layer_movement` (joined to `contract_version_balance`, `contract`, `customer`, `legal_entity`). ## 43. F-RPS-CUTOFF-R1 — an explicit as-of report read keeps its supplied cutoff; the read basis is stored with every run (`known_at_basis`); READ-1 reconciled with L6-3-Q-19; pre-parameter runs; the registered path's record-time clock; the persisted line time (Codex 1826 / 1838 / 1845 / 1849; D-98 candidate 112; docs first: 04 1.54, SCREENS_B 1.18, dev-guide 1.39)

**Finding (Codex packet production-20260920-1826, `PRODUCTION-F-RPS-POS117-CUTOFF-SOURCE-caab587c.md`, SHA-256 `04d7ea56…`; relayed ~11:50 PDT).** A balance report requested as of an earlier checkpoint could select a LATER contract version: READ-1 (§19; this record :456) requires `contract_version.known_at <= known_at[after_seq]`, but `balance_aging.py:355–359` passed the run's `known_at` through `tie_outs.version_cutoff`, which widened it to the job's transaction time (L6-3-Q-19) — `v10 <= k < v11 <= r` selects v11, and the money of the POS-117 witness is identical in March, so the caab587c test could not have caught it. Codex's four shapes: (i) new seed/witness where the bases differ; (ii) a stored per-run basis; (iii) a runner-only hack (rejected: the API must carry it); (iv) retroactive doc change (rejected).

**Preflight (Codex packet 1838; relayed ~12:00 PDT).** `framework._resolve` copies the given parameters, defaults `known_at` to `uow.now`, parses a supplied value and then UNCONDITIONALLY injects `parameters["known_at"]` when the catalogue offers the key; `_Resolved` / `report_run` persist only the normalised value; `report_params` carried no provenance. So explicitness must be decided where request provenance exists — from the CALLER-SUPPLIED parameters in `_resolve` — and persisted for run, rerun and explain; never inferred from a timestamp or from the normalised key's presence. **Decision (supervisor, D-98 candidate 112 "CUTOFF-R1 basis"): option (ii).**

**Reconciliation READ-1 vs L6-3-Q-19.** L6-3-Q-19 (`docs/reviews/loop/sprint/L6-3.md:306`, a sprint record): `contract_version.known_at` was the computation's record cutoff (the later of the application clock and the transaction timestamp, `bundles.record_cutoff`, L3-1-Q-25); a run's default `known_at` is the application clock; so reports read versions recorded by the later of the run's `known_at` and the job's transaction time — right for a defaulted `known_at`, which in a FrozenClock world lags the server stamp on the version. READ-1 (this record §19): a checkpoint read is as of the ledger's server stamp — an explicit cutoff that must hold exactly. Both hold when the basis is a stored run parameter: `record` (defaulted `known_at`; L6-3-Q-19 unchanged) and `historical` (supplied `known_at`; READ-1).

**Docs first (governed; the first commit of this slice).** 04 §16.9 API-S-ReportRunCreate `parameters` gains `known_at_basis` (`record` | `historical`, default `record`; stored per D-87; `historical` when the caller supplied `known_at`, `record` when it defaulted; `historical` without `known_at` is 422; a run stored before the parameter carries no key and reads, reruns and explains on the `record` basis — the stored value, never inferred from a stored `known_at`) — row 1.54 + header entry (4 cells, the log's width). SCREENS_B RPT-R-01 parameter keys gain `known_at_basis`; RV-03 shows `known_at` with its basis ("As of the supplied cutoff" / "Recorded by the run" / "basis: record (pre-parameter run)") — row 1.18 + header date (4 cells). Dev-guide DG-AK-41 gains the rule sentence — two bases stored per run; READ-1 `historical`; L6-3-Q-19 `record`; dashboards current reads; run, reruns and explain resolve the same versions; pre-parameter runs stay on the record basis; the runner's checkpoint report run validates its record-time `known_at` on the record-time clock (no substitution, advance or bypass; journal runs keep the application clock) — row 1.39 + header entry (3 cells). Numbers assigned by the supervisor.

**Applied (code).**
- `catalogue`: `KNOWN_AT_BASIS_KEY = "known_at_basis"`, `RECORD_BASIS`, `HISTORICAL_BASIS`; `PARAMETER_SCHEMAS["known_at_basis"] = enum(record, historical)`; `parameters_schema` appends the key after `known_at` for every definition (56) — so `ipe_logic.parameters` follows automatically.
- `framework`: `resolve_basis(given, *, known_at_given)` decides the basis from the CALLER-SUPPLIED parameters (a supplied basis is honoured; `historical` without a `known_at` is refused with the finding `parameters.known_at_basis` "known_at_basis 'historical' needs a known_at to hold."; `record` with a `known_at` is an explicit compatibility read; otherwise `historical` iff the caller supplied `known_at`); `_resolve` stores it as `parameters["known_at_basis"]` (a schema key, D-87) and sets `_Resolved.historical`; `report_params(run)` derives `ReportParams.historical` from the STORED key — one path for `run_report`, `rerun` (which copies the stored parameters without re-resolving: `_Resolved(parameters=dict(original["parameters"]), …)`) and `explain_cell` (`report_params(row)`). The interim job-payload flag of the first attempt is gone. **Pre-parameter runs (Codex 1845):** a stored run without the key reads `historical=False` — the record basis — and its rerun stores no key either; nothing is inferred from the stored `known_at`.
- `tie_outs.effective_cutoff(known_at, transaction_at, *, historical=False)` — the pure two-basis rule (`TypeError` on a missing transaction timestamp); `version_cutoff(session, known_at, *, historical=False)` applies it. `ReportParams.historical: bool = False` (trailing defaulted field; positional constructions unaffected).
- Consumers of `version_cutoff` (13 call sites, all reviewed): the ten `params`-based calls pass `historical=params.historical` — `balance_aging.py` (the finding's site), `contract_balance_rollforward.py`, `contract_balances.py`, `contract_cost_rollforward.py`, `contract_history.py` (two, incl. the multi-line predicate), `revenue_from_opening_liability.py`, `revenue_waterfall.py` (whose two `latest_versions` reads share the one cutoff), `rpo.py`, `rpo_rollforward.py`; the two `dashboard.py` calls are current reads at `uow.now` and stay on the record basis; the remaining site is `version_cutoff` itself.
- Migration 0048 `REPORT_DEFINITION_SEED` regenerated from `catalogue.seed_statement()` per DG-MIG-07 (rev 1.34, D-98 98: until the first production database exists a definition-text change regenerates the 0048 literal — as F-LMG did at be87eda5; 56 definitions, one line each; the drift oracle `test_revision_seed_equals_fresh_rendering` green). No new Alembic revision; `docs/api/openapi.json` verified byte-identical (`parameters` is an open object).
- **The registered path's record-time clock (Codex 1845).** The runner's checkpoint report run passes the checkpoint's `known_at` — the ledger's server stamp, `SELECT clock_timestamp()` after the last item's commit (`WorkspaceAdapter._record`) — so it is stored `historical` and keeps READ-1's cutoff. But `RunnerWorkspace.uow` captured `now` from the workspace's application `FrozenClock`, still set to the last item's business time (DG-AK-41), and `framework._resolve` refuses a supplied `known_at` later than `uow.now` (`KNOWN_AT_FUTURE`): every checkpoint report would have been refused 422 on the registered path. Fix: `RunnerWorkspace.uow(principal, *, clock=None)` captures `now` from a caller-supplied clock; `WorkspaceJobs.report_run` opens the run's unit of work, and runs its job (`_run(job_id, *, clock=None)`), on a `FrozenClock` fixed at ONE server read (`_server_clock`, the same read that stamps checkpoints) — the record-time clock. Not a business-date substitution (the cutoff stays the stamp), not a cutoff advance (`known_at` is what the checkpoint captured), not a bypass (the framework's check runs, against record time); the workspace clock is not moved (the next item finds it in place); `journal_run` is unchanged (its cutoff is not validated against `uow.now`; its postings keep the application clock).

**Persisted line time (Codex 1849) — the interim draft's provenance claim WITHDRAWN and corrected.** The interim §43 draft (relayed in this lane's ~12:20 PDT report; never committed) said `subledger_line.recorded_at` "is the server default at insert (`tables/subledger.py:121`)". That is FALSE. `subledger.post` (`domain/journals/subledger.py:363–374`) stamps every line `recorded_at = uow.now` at :369 — the application clock, captured once per unit of work (DG-KRN-UOW-01): in production the request's server time, under the runner the item's business time (DG-AK-41). The DB default `now()` of 0040:426–428 (`_timestamp`) never applies to a writer row; `recorded_at` is a sealed T-SL-04 member (`LINE_MEMBERS`: every stored column but `dr_cr`). `contract_event.recorded_at` is server-assigned by `tg_contract_event__insert` (DB-08; 0038:243) — a different path — `contract_version.known_at` is the greatest event `recorded_at` (`computation.persist`), and the checkpoint stamps are `clock_timestamp()`. So under the historical basis the two cutoffs run on two clocks: versions on the SERVER clock (exact after this slice), lines — API-C-10 `recorded_at <= known_at` in `balance_aging.py:249`, `views._consolidated`, `subledger.line_statement`, `queries.py:1627 / 1719` — on the APPLICATION clock. Consequence, in Codex's terms: a line whose posting COMMITTED after the stamp, with an application clock BEFORE it, in the SAME effective period, is admitted by a historical read as of the stamp. In production the window is the request's duration (`uow.now` precedes its commit); under the runner it is unbounded (business clock ≪ wall clock), so a historical rerun as of an earlier checkpoint would admit every later journal posting. What holds today: the runner's checkpoint reads execute at checkpoint time and see only committed lines; version selection is exact. What does not: record-time exclusion of LINES for later reads and reruns on the historical basis. **Not fixed in this slice — returned.** The reconciliation needs a record-time stamp on the line: a writer change to a sealed member with governed text (DG-KRN-TIME-01; 04 T-SL-04 / API-C-10 / the :2196 mapping-effectivity clock, which would move from business to wall time under the runner) and other lanes' pins (F-CTR `tests/domain/contracts/test_void.py:827–911`, `tests/api/test_subledger_api.py:111`). No migration was created (the `transaction_timestamp()`-in-`post` option needs none; a new server-stamped column would be a NEW revision after the last landed, never inserted into 0061–0064). the reconciliation is ruled as option (b) and scheduled as frps5. **Codex 1922 refinements (relayed ~12:24 PDT; adopted here, binding on frps5):** (1) the "existing rows nullable; the historical predicate falls back to `recorded_at` for NULL" wording is WITHDRAWN — a NULL → `recorded_at` fallback cannot close historical correctness (line `recorded_at`, posting / line / seal `created_at` and `sealed_at` all use `uow.now`; event / version time can predate a deferred posting; no persisted server timestamp exists to substitute): legacy rows need a PROVABLE RECONSTRUCTION of their record time or an EXPLICIT NAMED REFUSAL wherever their unknown record time could affect the requested historical result (e.g. a historical read whose window contains legacy rows refuses by name rather than silently using the application clock) — designed into frps5; (2) the new column's clock is specified precisely — PostgreSQL `transaction_timestamp()` / `now()` is the transaction-START time, `clock_timestamp()` the call time, and neither is a commit-order guarantee — so frps5 states the chosen semantics and TESTS the long-transaction / checkpoint boundary before claiming arbitrary later-commit exclusion; business-time mapping and seals preserved; (3) the strict XFAIL carried by this slice is UNRESOLVED EVIDENCE, not a fix. **Codex 1927 (sealed design note `PRODUCTION-F-RPS-Q3-LINE-TIME-DESIGN-fe17794d.md`, SHA-256 `38d85a04…`, against main fe17794d; (3) adding the table column must NOT silently extend the dynamic `LINE_MEMBERS` seal payload — the column is seal-excluded by construction and a test asserts the seal payload unchanged; (4) database-authored provenance and immutability for the new seal-excluded metadata under application-role controls — the record-time column is set by the database / writer path only, with no application-role UPDATE (RLS / grant / trigger as the schema convention dictates, documented in 04); (5) legacy rows → explicit named refusal or proven reconstruction (1922); (6) transaction-start / write time distinguished from historical committed-population evidence, the long-transaction / checkpoint boundary tested before any later-commit-exclusion claim;
