**Codex 1952 (packets `PRODUCTION-F-RPS-CUTOFF-BASIS-SOURCE-c313b692.md`, `-CUTOFF-DBTEST-SOURCE-e7c6dc77.md`, `-REPORT-CLOCK-SOURCE-c313b692.md`; relayed ~12:50 PDT) — four items folded before READY (tests + record + the dev-guide sentence).**
1. **RESIDUAL, stays OPEN — the same-version claim is qualified.** DG-AK-41 / §43 claimed version identity across the run, its reruns and `GET /explain/report-runs/<built-in function id>/cell`. For a `historical`-basis run the CUTOFF is preserved (READ-1 keeps the stored cutoff) — but, per Codex 2000 (packet production-20260920-2000; design note `PRODUCTION-F-RPS-FRPS3B-SOURCE-BINDING-DESIGN-e7c6dc77.md`, SHA-256 `20b9a42f…`), the original SOURCES are not yet bound, with two committed-source witnesses: (a) a deferred computation can persist a higher version LATER whose `known_at` is still below the fixed cutoff (`computation.persist` derives `known_at` from `max(event.recorded_at)`, not from persistence time: an event admitted before K, computed later), so a later read at the same K selects the new version; (b) an ordinary admitted customer-segment A → B edit changes RPO `CUSTOMER_SEGMENT` row keys through live metadata joins while version ids, cutoff and amounts stay fixed — the saved dataset keeps row A while the registered cell explanation requeries B and refuses A as unknown. So the claim is limited to the requested cutoff. **Codex 2015 (relayed ~13:20 PDT):** the governed texts — the DG-AK-41 sentence, the dev-guide header entry, row 1.39 and ENGINE_SPEC_B row 1.32 — now state, and this record states: (1) the historical basis preserves the REQUESTED CUTOFF (the stored `known_at_basis` / cutoff per run); (2) whether the original sources are the same remains UNPROVED until frps3b binds the consumed version history / trace / contributors; (3) deferred computation below K and mutable customer grouping apply to the historical basis too; (4) the scoped historical rerun tests (`test_rerun_version_provenance_db.py`, `test_historical_line_cutoff_db.py`) are AUTHORED DB cases (NOT RUN) with the intended assertion scope — a result exists only when the admitted candidate-bound execution retains it — and are not a general reproduction guarantee (wording corrected in place per Codex 2052, §43.3) — none of those texts asserts version identity or a reproduction for a historical-basis rerun. NOT true even for the cutoff on the `record` basis: `version_cutoff` computes `max(stored known_at, THIS request's transaction_timestamp())`, so a later eligible version of the same group / book / period enters a rerun or a cell explanation while the original output stays saved; neither the resolved cutoff nor the selected version ids are frozen by the basis flag. Dev-guide 1.39 (DG-AK-41 sentence, the 1.39 row and the header entry) now reads in those terms: the requested cutoff preserved for `historical`-basis runs, same original sources UNPROVED until frps3b, deferred computation / mutable grouping applying to the historical basis too, the scoped tests authored DB cases, NOT RUN, with the intended assertion scope; `record`-basis reruns / explanations additionally resolve against the current transaction time — provenance binding pending frps3b. §43's "one path for run_report, rerun and explain" describes the CODE path (one derivation of the basis), not version identity across time; this paragraph supersedes it. **Witness at the real boundaries** (`tests/domain/reports/test_rerun_version_provenance_db.py`, DB-bound, NOT RUN): `contract_history` on WLD-K-01 (rows are obligation versions, `version:<external id>:<version no>:<obligation key>`); a billing appended and computed AFTER the original runs makes version 2; `POST /report-runs` → worker → `POST /report-runs/<built-in function id>/rerun` → worker → `GET …/data`. The `historical` run (created with `known_at` = a server stamp taken before version 2, on the record-time clock) reruns to its original row keys and control totals — an AUTHORED DB case (NOT RUN) with the intended assertion scope (event and computation both after the stamp); a result exists only when the admitted candidate-bound execution retains it; not a general reproduction guarantee (Codex 2000's deferred-computation case is frps3b's control, stated in the module docstring; wording corrected in place per Codex 2052, §43.3); the `record`-basis run's rerun is asserted to keep its original version rows and is carried as `xfail(strict=True)` naming this residual and frps3b — red today by design, unresolved evidence, not a fix. **frps3b — CUTOFF-R1 provenance binding (design; implementation dispatched after frps3 lands):** a run persists the resolved cutoff (`resolved_cutoff`, the `effective_cutoff` the job actually applied) and the selected version ids per book (`selected_version_ids`) alongside its output; `rerun` and `explain_cell` of a SUCCEEDED run read that binding — versions by stored id, lines and events by the stored cutoff — instead of re-resolving against the current transaction time, so a saved output and its explanation carry their original sources; the current-view compatibility (a NEW run on the record basis reads the current state, as today) is preserved; the binding is written by the job at persist time (04 §16.9 / T-RPT-02 columns or a run-scoped table → a NEW Alembic revision numbered after the last landed at its landing; docs first; DG-MIG numbering per the supervisor); reruns of pre-binding runs refuse by name or re-resolve with an explicit "unbound" marker — to be ruled in the dispatch. **Codex 2000 additions to the frps3b design:** the binding freezes the actually CONSUMED version history, trace and contributor identities — not merely a timestamp or a new "latest" query — and the material grouping / label values (or verifiable original cell / contributor evidence) as well as ids, so a saved output, its same-source rerun and its original-cell explanation retain the captured population, row keys and contributors even when amounts match; current authorization checks stay on every read; a separately requested current-view run may use the new population. Two saved-run / registered-explain controls to design and implement: (a) the later-version case PLUS a version persisted after the original run but with `known_at` below its fixed cutoff (deferred computation); (b) an RPO metadata-only customer-segment A → B edit with unchanged financial inputs, versions, currency and totals — the registered explainers are RPO and `revenue_waterfall` (`contract_balances` is not one); legacy runs without original provenance are qualified or refused under the governed contract — never reconstructed from today's rows and presented as the original. Q-3 line time (frps5) and RPS-SNAP (frps4) stay separate.
2. **DG-MIG-07 transition (0048 seed).** Only 0048's INSERT seed literal changes (56 `known_at_basis` property additions and the 56 IPE-name additions the regeneration carried); the schema is unchanged. An existing, unreconstructed database keeps its closed `parameters_schema`: an explicit `known_at_basis` is rejected as a key outside the schema (T-RPT-01 rule 1), and an inferred `historical` basis is NOT persisted (the key is not in `properties`, so `_resolve` stores nothing) and later reads as `record`. The pre-first-production lane / test database rebuild is therefore essential; native evidence for this change binds to FRESH catalogue contents — the integrated batch's `test-pg` / `ci` on a fresh `erev_test`. No database action by this lane.
3. **POS-117 test strengthened.** The identity assertions ran separate selector queries after the builder, and both bases yield equal March money, so a builder-only loss of `historical=params.historical` could have passed. `test_platform_pos117_balance_aging_db.py` now installs a forwarding observer over `tie_outs.version_cutoff` and `tie_outs.latest_versions` (records each call's arguments and results, forwards to the real functions) and asserts on what `balance_aging.build` REALLY used: one `version_cutoff(known_at, historical=True)` call returning the strict cutoff, `latest_versions` receiving it, and the builder's own selection statement resolving to the READ-1 identity set; the record-basis control shows `historical=False`, the widened cutoff and the widened set. Four cells, the 70 handler steps, the key, seq 7 and the tie-out unchanged. The strict-xfail line exclusion stays frps5.
4. **Clock tests.** The collaborator tests' `create_run` stand-in copies the future-time guard; added: the REAL `framework._resolve` boundary with the POS-117 clock shape (CPU; the entity-scope read stands in) — refused with `KNOWN_AT_FUTURE` at the business clock, accepted and stored `historical` with the stamp unchanged at the record-time clock — and the DB-bound `tests/domain/reports/test_report_run_clock_db.py` (NOT RUN): `POST /report-runs` with `known_at` = a fresh `clock_timestamp()` → 422 `parameters.known_at` / `KNOWN_AT_FUTURE` on the business clock, 202 and `known_at_basis = historical` with the stamp unchanged once the clock is the record-time clock. ## 44. frps3b — CUTOFF-R1 consumed-source binding (Codex 2131 dispatch; design contract PRODUCTION-F-RPS-FRPS3B-SOURCE-BINDING-DESIGN-e7c6dc77.md, SHA-256 `20b9a42f…`; docs first: 04 1.55, ENGINE_SPEC_B 1.33 / S15-R-24, SCREENS_B 1.19, dev-guide 1.47; folded through Codex 2137 / 2154 / 2202 / 2216 / 2225 / 2233 / 2242 / 2245 / 2301 / 2315 / 2318 / 2331)

**Dispatch (supervisor, ~14:30 PDT).** On the integrated head main 874eabc1 (merged into sprint/l17 as 0823ddf6; pre-check `git merge-tree --write-tree --name-only 874eabc1 38468fe9` → tree `f1a22538…`, clean): bind with every run the ACTUAL consumed version history, the traces / contributor identities and the mutable grouping / label values so that rerun and explain resolve against the BOUND sources, not today's rows; cover (a) a deferred computation below the original cutoff, (b) later eligible versions, (c) a customer-segment A → B change; compare the STORED original output with the actual rerun AND the REGISTERED explain routes (RPO and `revenue_waterfall`); preserve authorization; legacy runs QUALIFIED or REFUSED by name, never reconstructed; decide the persistence in the docs-first step; the migration number from main's versions directory at commit time; frps4 / frps5 separate. Subsequent folds required before READY: Codex 2137 (merge current main; number the migration only after ENG-C6's 0064; prefer the D-87 parameters path if faithful), 2154 (1)–(5), 2202 (every registered builder), 2216 (A)–(E), 2225 (retained evidence must drive an actual rebuild), 2233 (pins → "0065"; function-count pin 78), 2242 (the committed replay is a self-comparison; wrong-file witness; add proof of real rebuilding and a detectable calculation mismatch) and 2245 (partial dispositions on ddeeee3b: (a) cutoff truncation, (b) label fallback, (c) journal-run candidates recorded as consumed, (d) configuration completeness not established) and 2301 ((a) F3B-TEST-SETUP-R1 invalid inventory-case setup; (b) the open declarations must name the actual input families; (c) ENG-C4's eighteenth builder when it lands; (d) 2245 stays part of READY) and 2315 (source closures verified at the merge 9a77db23; three authored DB-test reachability defects; operative docs alignment; the frps3c scope statement) and 2318 (F3B-TEST-FAILED-CUTOFF-R1: the FAILED-original case's cutoff column vs parameter).

**Design (docs first, commit `8a08cf90`; amended in place before landing by `ddeeee3b`, `9a2cb39e`, `36f42e4f` and `69de537b`).** A fixed cutoff alone does not freeze a live run's sources (§43.1; Codex 2000's two witnesses). Every LIVE run therefore binds, once at SUCCEEDED, the sources its build actually consumed in a NEW nullable jsonb column `report_run.source_binding` (04 T-RPT-02 rev 1.55; `binding_version` 1): `strategy` (`adapter` | `retained_inputs` | `open`), `report_version`, the EXACT effective `cutoff` (tz-aware instant, microseconds), `versions` (book → the contract-version ids consumed), `labels` (kind → id → the consumed value: `customer_segment`, `customer_name`, `product_family`, `product_name`, `product_revenue_category` as each builder declares), `members` (kind → the non-version rows consumed: `subledger_line`; `journal_run` — the CONTRIBUTING runs, captured under the sum's own book / entity / period predicates; `api_client`), `evidence` (kind → the input facts a retained-inputs builder consumed), `open` (the dimensions the builder's contract leaves unbound — declared, never silently narrowed), `dataset` (`output_sha256`, `format`) and `row_keys`. **Persistence decision (Codex 2137 asked for the D-87 stored-run-parameter path where faithful):** the D-87 `parameters` CANNOT carry the binding faithfully — the actual consumed ids, labels and evidence are known only AFTER the build (a version can land between creation and build) and T-RPT-02 `parameters` is immutable after creation (not among the IM-S updatable columns; the 0048 transition trigger refuses it) — so a job-written column is the only path that records what was actually consumed; it is also consistent with closed request parameters and the as-locked scope; the precedent for job-written run provenance is `ledger_heads`. Rerun copies the original's binding into the new run, whose build resolves against it (an `open` builder's rerun carries none and captures its own); a cell explanation resolves against it; a row key the original did not hold is refused by name; a binding that lacks a label or evidence the bound read needs is refused by name (never completed from today's rows); a SUCCEEDED live run without a binding (created before the revision) is REFUSED by name on rerun (409 `invalid-transition`) and on cell explanation (404 `not-found`) — never reconstructed — except for an `open` builder, whose every rerun is a new live evaluation; as-locked runs are unchanged (their frozen dataset is their source, S15-R-19) and carry no binding. Governed texts: 04 1.55 (T-RPT-02 column + IM-S class list; §16.9 API-S-ReportRun `sources`; §16.9 rerun; §16.11 explain; log row), ENGINE_SPEC_B 1.33 (new rule S15-R-24), SCREENS_B 1.19 (RV-03 "Sources" copy per kind; the rerun button's copy on legacy / failed / open runs; SB-R-07), dev-guide 1.47 (DG-AK-41: the frps3b sentence discharged by this slice's code, the authored DB cases NOT RUN in the lane). Numbers assigned by the lane per descending union; every row at its header's width.

**Applied (code; commits `83ebb583`, `ddeeee3b`, `9a2cb39e`, `36f42e4f`, `69de537b`).**
- `builders/__init__.py`: `SourceBinding` (frozen: `cutoff`, `versions`, `labels`, `members`, `evidence`, `open`, `row_keys`, `strategy`, `report_version`, `dataset`; `to_stored` / `from_stored` — a foreign `binding_version` raises; the cutoff stored as `outputs.utc_text` and restored as the exact instant; `version_ids`, `member_ids`, `label`, `summary` with `kind` bound / retained / open and `bound` false for `open`), `SourceCollector` (records cutoff / versions / labels / members / evidence; `binding(row_keys=, strategy=, open=, report_version=, dataset=)`), constants `ADAPTER` = `adapter`, `RETAINED` = `retained_inputs`, `OPEN` = `open`; `ReportParams.binding` (a bound run) and `ReportParams.sources` (a live build's collector).
- `tie_outs`: `cutoff_for` (the BOUND cutoff, never the current transaction time, else `version_cutoff` recorded); `versions_for` (the bound ids, never a new "latest" query, else `latest_versions` once and recorded); `label_for` (the bound value — a binding that holds no value for the key raises `invalid-transition` `INCOMPLETE_BINDING` by name, never the live value; live: the value recorded); `record_consumed_versions`, `bound_version_where`, `in_versions`, `bound_member_where`, `record_members`, `record_evidence`, `bound_evidence` (missing evidence refuses by name); `journal_revenue(..., params=)` binds the tie-out's journal-run membership; `balances_at(..., params=)`.
- Builders: the ten `version_cutoff` sites → `cutoff_for`; the seven `balances_at` calls pass `params`; `rpo.load(..., params=)` restricts the version history to the bound ids and binds `customer_segment` / `customer_name` / `product_family` / `product_name`; `revenue_waterfall` binds its versions, its recognized subledger lines (members) and `customer_name` / `product_revenue_category`; `contract_history` restricts and records its versions; `disaggregation` binds the tie-out membership; `api_client_inventory` (strategy `retained_inputs`): a live build records the consumed client rows, entity codes and creator display names as evidence (`_live_inputs` → `_json_safe` → `record_evidence`) and a bound run feeds that evidence to the SAME row construction (`_from_evidence`), reading no live row.
- `framework`: `SOURCE_CONTRACTS` — every one of the 17 `BUILDERS` states its strategy, bound dimensions and OPEN dimensions (asserted equal to `BUILDERS` at import); `KIND_*` lifecycle constants incl. `KIND_OPEN`; `run_kind`; `require_bound` (refuses `legacy_unbound` and an unreadable binding by name, except an `open` builder's legacy run); `require_original_row`; `rerun` copies the binding (none for `open`); `report_params(run)` derives `ReportParams.binding`; `run_report` ALWAYS rebuilds through the builder — a fresh `SourceCollector` for an original run, the binding for a rerun — and persists the binding at SUCCEEDED (the basis cutoff recorded even for a builder that selects no versions; NULL for as-locked runs); the output replay (`_replay_retained`, `_Retained`, `_media_of`) is DELETED; `explain_cell` refuses by name and runs the explainer bound; `run_outs` → `sources_out` (API-S-ReportRun `sources`: `ReportRunSourcesOut` with `bound`, `kind`, `strategy`, `cutoff`, `versions`, `labels`, `members`, `rows`, `open`); `db/transitions.py` admits `source_binding`; `db/tables/reports.py` gains the column; `privacy/classification.py` classifies it (R02); `support/rows.report_run_values` carries it NULL.
- API: `docs/api/openapi.json` and `frontend/src/lib/api/schema.d.ts` regenerated with `make openapi` in `83ebb583`; unchanged by the folds (`test_openapi_export` green in the CPU gate).
- **Migration `0065_report_run_source_binding.py`** (`down_revision` `0064` = ENG-C6's `0064_subledger_line_event`; the tree's single Alembic head). A provisional `0064_report_run_source_binding.py` had been committed (`817ca725`) and was REVERTED (`934ee289`) on the supervisor's instruction (Codex 2137: 0064 is ENG-C6's; never reserve a provisional number); after ENG-C6 landed on main 7bf044a5 the revision was re-added as 0065 (`58890c1f`; role literal `57f9010c`): `ALTER TABLE erev.report_run ADD COLUMN source_binding jsonb NULL`; `ck_report_run__source_binding` (a JSON object when present); `GRANT UPDATE (source_binding)` to `erev_app` (IM-S grants UPDATE on listed columns only, 04 §1.5); the DB-03 transition trigger function `tg_report_run__transition()` REPLACED with the 0048 body plus `source_binding` among the columns that may change while QUEUED / RUNNING (`CREATE OR REPLACE` keeps trigger and grants — no new function: the `erev` function-count pin stays 78; Codex 2216 (E) / 2233); downgrade restores the 0048 body exactly, revokes the grant, drops the check and the column (DG-MIG-04). The file imports only `migration_ops` and spells the role as a literal (DG-MIG-12); no enum, no seed (DG-MIG-07 uninvolved), no backfill: every run created before the revision is unbound. Head pins `tests/pg/test_migrations.py:111` and `tests/api/test_me.py:192` → `"0065"` in the separate commit `e747ff2d` (Codex 2233: at 57f9010c both still read "0064").

**Per-builder source contracts (Codex 2202; `framework.SOURCE_CONTRACTS`, pinned by `test_every_registered_live_builder_states_its_source_contract`).**

| Builder | Strategy | Bound (a rerun / explanation reads exactly these) | Explicitly OPEN (declared in the binding and `sources.open`; frps3c) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `rpo` | adapter | exact cutoff; the WHOLE consumed version history; schedule lines as children; labels customer_segment / customer_name, product_family / product_name; policies / elections resolved at the bound cutoff | consumed-configuration completeness (entity calendar assignment, period definitions, entity attributes read current) |
| `rpo_rollforward` | adapter | as `rpo` at both ends | consumed-configuration completeness |
| `revenue_waterfall` | adapter | exact cutoff; versions; schedule lines; recognized subledger-line membership (the cell contributors); the revenue tie-out's CONTRIBUTING journal runs (captured under the sum's scope); labels customer_name, product_revenue_category; policies / elections | consumed-configuration completeness |
| `contract_history` | adapter | exact cutoff; the consumed versions; cause events as children | consumed-configuration completeness |
| `contract_balances` | adapter | exact cutoff; versions; balances as children | subledger-line membership; consumed-configuration completeness |
| `contract_balance_rollforward` | adapter | exact cutoff; versions; balances at both ends | subledger-line membership; consumed-configuration completeness |
| `contract_cost_rollforward` | adapter | exact cutoff; versions; cost balances | subledger-line membership; consumed-configuration completeness |
| `revenue_from_opening_liability` | adapter | exact cutoff; versions; opening balances | subledger-line membership; consumed-configuration completeness |
| `disaggregation` | adapter | exact cutoff; the revenue tie-out's CONTRIBUTING journal runs; policies / elections | subledger-line membership; product grouping labels; consumed-configuration completeness |
| `api_client_inventory` | retained_inputs | exact cutoff; api-client membership; retained INPUT evidence — the consumed client rows, entity codes and creator display names — fed to the real builder logic on a same-source rebuild (no live row read; a calculation change is a CTL-029 mismatch) | — |
| `legacy_contract_history_export` | open | exact cutoff only (the basis) — a rerun is a NEW live evaluation labelled `open` | contract / obligation rows incl. predecessor obligation rows of legacy conversions |
| `latest_contract_status` | open | exact cutoff only | latest contract-status rows (contract / version / status reads) |
| `legacy_latest_contract_export` | open | exact cutoff only | contract / obligation rows incl. predecessor obligation rows of legacy conversions |
| `legacy_je_summary` | open | exact cutoff only | SUBLEDGER-line population (GROSS / DELTA applicability; enabled LEGACY books) |
| `migration_reconciliation` | open | exact cutoff only | migration reconciliation, profile and exception mappings |
| `je_population` (ENG-C6) | open | exact cutoff only | journal population: the independent total side and the history side |
| `out_of_period_register` (ENG-C6) | open | exact cutoff only | subledger-line population and postings, ordered T-SL-12 event sets, computation / approval references, whole-group per-book cause proof (missing evidence distinct from a proved zero) |

The `open` declarations name each builder's ACTUAL input families per Codex 2301's frps3c baseline map — a frps3c rebuild is from those inputs, never from substituted journal lines; the interim `open` / new-evaluation status waives nothing of the full same-source objective. Nothing is bound "by a JSON object existing": an `open` binding says `strategy: open`, `sources.bound` is false, `sources.kind` is `open`, and the rerun button reads "Rerun (new evaluation)" (SCREENS_B RV-03).

**Contract (ADAPTER; `framework.SOURCE_CONTRACTS`).** Bound: the exact cutoff (`tie_outs.cutoff_for` replaces `version_cutoff` in `build()` — a bound rerun applies the ORIGINAL instant, never the current transaction time); the contract versions selected per (group, period) — `_selected_versions(..., params=, book_code=)` restricts its candidate rows with `bound_version_where` under a binding (never a new selection) and records the selected ids with `record_consumed_versions` on a live build; the historical combination-group membership candidates — `_encoded_contracts(..., params=)` records the candidate contract ids as `members.contract` on a live build and a bound run uses exactly those (T-CON-04 as-of identities preserved; C4-PP-R3 unchanged); stored traces (`load_trace` by version id), obligation rows and contract events by ref are children of the bound versions; the sum-node identity checks (`check_identity`, S15-R-13) run on the rebuilt read. OPEN (declared in the binding and `sources.open`; both remain REQUIRED frps3c work — Codex production-20260921-0020 (b)): the subledger-line REFERENCE population (`_references`, read live by `recorded_at <= known_at`) — the bound version ids restrict ELIGIBILITY, they do not freeze the live reference-to-period association (which (entity, period) a version is read for) — and the consumed-configuration completeness (entity codes, calendars). Two instants are in play and are kept distinct: the historical membership read (`_encoded_contracts`, `valid_from_known_at <= known_at`) and the reference read use the run's stored `params.known_at` (copied verbatim to a rerun), while the version selection uses the EFFECTIVE version cutoff (`cutoff_for`: the bound exact instant of a rerun; on a record-basis original the transaction-time cutoff, which can differ from `params.known_at`). Live behaviour unchanged ON THE UNBOUND RECORD-BASIS PATH: the same selection, traces, event types, identity checks and refusals — the live read merely records what it consumed. An explicit HISTORICAL read is intentionally different from the pre-composition builder: it called `version_cutoff(session, params.known_at)` on the record basis (the later of `known_at` and the transaction time) regardless of `known_at_basis`, whereas `cutoff_for` applies the shared historical-cutoff rule (READ-1: the supplied `known_at` exactly; F-RPS-CUTOFF-R1, §43) — the eighteenth builder now adopts the same two-basis rule as the other seventeen (Codex production-20260921-0031 (3)). **Migration 0065 needs no change** (the column is generic jsonb); **no test needs a change except this lane's registry-size pin (17 → 18) and the new contract assertions** in `test_source_binding.py` (15 passed on the fold tree) — these new assertions cover DECLARATIONS, the registry count and the open qualifiers; they are NOT non-empty bound-rebuild execution evidence for the C4 adapter (no C4 bound rerun is executed in this lane; Codex production-20260921-0020 (c)); C4's own unit / domain / engine tests are untouched (`framework.BUILDERS[pp.CODE] is pp.build` holds). ENGINE_SPEC_B S15-R-24's adapter list names the builder and its open dimensions.

## 45. frps3c — retained / rebuildable ACTUAL input families for every incomplete family (DESIGN-FIRST; design contract `PRODUCTION-F-RPS-FRPS3C-INPUT-FAMILIES-DESIGN.md`, SHA-256 `53340c47c6bd71084887121ee5720a9b907681da1ce811a76c235ce7fb427839`; READY-FOR-REVIEW, not for merge)

Baseline: sprint/l17 at `36d9c0c9` = merge of main `0cb36c14` (the lane's own landing `010a6a9c` + the docs-lane commits after it; pre-check `git merge-tree --write-tree --name-only 0cb36c14 4859663a` → tree `0f7fccd8…`, no conflict; `uv sync` rc=0, typecheck rc=0, lint rc=0 — the merge committed inside the guard).

**Deliverable 1 (this commit, docs-first):** the design contract `docs/reviews/loop/prod/PRODUCTION-F-RPS-FRPS3C-INPUT-FAMILIES-DESIGN.md` — for each builder × incomplete family: (a) what the live build consumes today with source lines (§1: `tie_outs.entities/calendars/book_of`; the balance-family and disaggregation subledger-line reads; C4's `_references` / `_selected_versions` / `_encoded_contracts`; the seven `open` builders' reads through `contract_history.population / latest_where / previous_versions`, `journals/views.legacy_view`, `je_population`'s line population and `audit_event` / `posting_ack` / `approval_decision` as-of histories, `out_of_period_register`'s lines / postings / T-SL-12 sets / cause proof, `migration/repository` readers); (b) RETAIN vs REBUILD per family with the reason (§0 table, §2): configuration → RETAIN `evidence.configuration` + `labels.entity_code` (rows mutate in place); subledger-line and journal-line populations → BIND-IDS `members.subledger_line` / `members.journal_line` / `members.journal_run` (immutable server-stamped rows; the waterfall precedent; the rebuild re-reads amounts by id); product / GL-account / display-name labels → RETAIN labels; C4's reference-to-period association → RETAIN `evidence.reference_periods` (the association map IS the consumed input; eligibility stays the bound ids); the three history-family exports → CHILD of bound versions (+ `record_consumed_versions`) → `adapter`; `legacy_je_summary` → ids + `evidence.legacy_books` + GL labels → `adapter`; `je_population` → ids + append-only histories as CHILD + labels → `adapter`; `out_of_period_register` → ids + cause-proof versions + labels → `adapter`; `migration_reconciliation` → RETAIN batch / rows / exception map (rows are re-generated, not appended) → `retained_inputs`; (c) storage (§3): additive kinds inside `binding_version` 1 — no reader fork, **no migration (0066 not needed; stated per slice)**; `sources.complete` additive; the jsonb size MEASUREMENT POINT and the child-table alternative as a Codex decision (§3.3); (d) rerun / explain and CTL-029 (§4): rebuild unchanged; a differing rebuild = calculation change or an absent bound row (detected) or an incomplete binding (refused by name); optional `BOUND_ROW_MISSING`; legacy runs of the six ex-`open` builders become refused by name; (e) C4 (§5): eligibility (bound ids) ≠ association (retained map); `params.known_at` (original live reads only) ≠ the effective cutoff (`cutoff_for`); (f) tests (§6): CPU witnesses per kind; DB "change after the run" witnesses per family, NOT RUN; (g) accounting neutrality (§7): reporting-only; flags F1 (LEGACY-book disablement retained), F2 (re-reconciliation retained), F3 (legacy-run refusal for the six) routed to Ray's G12 review; (h) slice plan (§8): 3c-1 configuration → 3c-2 line members + product labels → 3c-3 C4 association → 3c-4 history-family trio → 3c-5 legacy_je_summary → 3c-6 je_population → 3c-7 out_of_period_register → 3c-8 migration_reconciliation → 3c-9 `sources.complete` + docs closure; each docs-first, gated under one pid-first slot, READY, Codex-reviewed. ### 45.1 frps3c design amended — Codex continuation map (production-20260921-0102, PRODUCTION-FRPS3C-INPUT-FAMILIES-010a6a9c.md SHA 5e685b3e…) and design review (production-20260921-0139, PRODUCTION-FRPS3C-DESIGN-REVIEW-fa64924e.md SHA 9cc45782… — read in full); new design SHA-256 `b2e9d57c805e9deb935fc476d44db149644d78c49631403858d7119897fa21bc` (238 lines); still DESIGN-FIRST for the later slices, 3c-1 GO

**Continuation map, folded (design §0.1, §1.2, §2.2–§2.7, §5, §8):** (1) scope = the incomplete families of all TEN adapters + all SEVEN `open` builders = 17 incomplete builders, `api_client_inventory`'s real retained-input rebuild preserved; (2) `tie_outs.balances_at` selects `customer.c.name` LIVE (`tie_outs.py:547, 553–563`) for `contract_balances`, `contract_balance_rollforward`, `revenue_from_opening_liability` → retained as labels (then superseded by D1, below); (3) C4 "requires the reference-to-entity/period/version ASSOCIATION, not merely the union of eligible version IDs" — quoted; the union is `versions`, the association is `evidence.reference_periods`; (4) preservation requirements written per family: predecessor rows by exact FK identity, the independent JE run totals from the bound runs, the ordered state / approval history, OOP's missing-proof vs proved-zero and `ordinal` event order; (5) the governing rule "rebuild from captured pre-aggregation inputs or proven immutable references, with no live fallback or cached-output substitute" (§0.1; `revenue_from_opening_liability` binds line ids and re-aggregates — never its `func.sum`).

**Design review fa64924e, folded (design §10 — controlling where §2–§4 differ):** **D1** — the balance family's consumed customer NAMES and the contract → customer ASSOCIATION are retained (`evidence.customer_association` {contract_id → customer_id | null}, `labels.customer_name` {customer_id → name | null}; NULL vs absent explicit at both levels; the bound `balances_at` joins neither `customer` nor `contract.customer_id` live) and MOVED INTO 3c-1. **D2** — `je_population`'s journal-state inputs are not cutoff-filtered children: `_transitions` (`:611–666`) reads the FULL ordered audit history without a cutoff; `state_as_of` (`:195–250`) consumes it plus the run's MUTABLE stored fields (`run_state`, `run_updated_at`, `run_approved_at`, `run_exported_at`, `run_acknowledged_at`, `run_cancelled_at`, `batch_acknowledged_at`) with an early stored-state branch and a full-history unknown-failure guard; `build` (`:491–504, 521–537, 543–568`) consumes the current `approval_request_id`, `total_debit_functional`, `functional_currency`, batch `external_id` / `acknowledged_at`; `db/transitions.py:516–552` permits those fields to change → bind the exact memberships (`members.journal_line / journal_run / journal_batch / audit_event` (full, ordered, untrimmed) `/ posting_ack / approval_decision`), retain the consumed mutable fields (`evidence.journal_runs`, `evidence.journal_batches`, request association included), RE-RUN `state_as_of` on a bound rerun (the `AsOfState` is never stored), keep the refusal rules and the independent tie-out. **D3** — immutable rows do not prove a closed child population: `subledger_line_event` (0064) has `ux_subledger_line_event__ordinal` and `ck …ordinal >= 1` but no closed-membership invariant → `out_of_period_register` binds the exact ordered T-SL-12 child identities (`members.subledger_line_event`) and retains the exact cause-proof inputs (`evidence.cause_proof` {computation_id → {version_id → cause_event_ids}}; an empty list is a proved zero, an absent computation is absent evidence → the original refusal reproduced); predecessor rows by exact FK identity; broad children-by-parent reads (obligation rows, balances, traces) must cite an enforceable closed-population invariant or bind ids — decided per converting slice with Codex, never assumed. **D4** — the named refusal `BOUND_INPUT_MISSING` compares EXACT identities, order and associations (never counts; a missing zero-value row or an offsetting pair leaves CTL-029 unchanged) before any figure; authorization / RLS unchanged — bound ids restrict WITHIN today's tenant / book / entity / original scope, never replace it; from 3c-1.

**Engineering decisions (supervisor, following Codex; design §10.5):** additive JSONB kinds, no forced 0066; explicit EMPTY vs ABSENT kinds and typed round-trip validation; measurement of size / latency / cost at scale before production closure, an explicit governed cap with the behaviour at it (proposed: `BINDING_TOO_LARGE` — the run succeeds UNBOUND and labelled so; never truncation), normalized storage revisited on measured evidence; journal histories bound + mutable fields retained, `state_as_of` rebuilt; legacy successful runs without enough binding refuse same-source rerun / explain by name with a separate current evaluation — for every converted builder incl. `migration_reconciliation`'s `retained_inputs`, failed-original behaviour preserved, no retroactive "reproducible" label; `sources.complete` = a VALID binding supplying every REQUIRED kind of the builder / version (a `SourceContract` declaration) with nothing open — never `bound: true` + `open: []` alone; accounting limits: reproducibility only — G12 not closed, no postings, no current-run amount change, F1–F3 open.

**Acceptance pattern per family (design §10.6):** non-empty original; a changed current input that changes a separate current evaluation; unchanged bound inputs through the real builder; a calculation-change mismatch; exact contributors, NULL / empty semantics, independent tie-outs, original financial assertions and named refusals preserved; a family's `open` removed only when its actual reader consumes complete retained / bound inputs.

**Next:** 3c-1 GO = configuration evidence for all TEN adapters + D1's customer association / names for the balance family + the `BOUND_INPUT_MISSING` refusal; docs-first with the supervisor's revision numbers; fail-first CPU tests; DB-bound NOT RUN; lint-guarded; slot-2 gates; record; READY-for-review → READY.

### 45.2 Codex production-20260921-0235 on the design amendment `53bd35e7` — D1–D4 SOURCE-ALIGNED; one §10.5 clause REJECTED and corrected (design SHA-256 now `815ab24db8eaeb1dd3572aab2e6bab2925397616ef12a09cc6fb761349fc9e34`)

Codex: "D1–D4 design amendment is source-ALIGNED … All TEN adapter+SEVEN open scope, real inventory rebuild and valid-required-kind completeness retained. This is docs-only, not a builder closure. Existing authorization for 3c-1 stands; proceed without another broad design gate." REJECTED: §10.5's proposal that an over-cap run "succeeds as an UNBOUND live evaluation" — S15-R-24 requires the consumed sources at completion, 04 T-RPT-02 writes `source_binding` once at SUCCEEDED and identifies a successful live run without it as pre-revision legacy, and the API `sources` kinds have no newly-successful-over-cap-unbound state. **Corrected in the design (§10.5, this commit):** when the required capture cannot complete within supported storage the run FAILS by name (`BINDING_TOO_LARGE`; `sources.kind` `failed_without_capture`) — never SUCCEEDED-unbound, never truncated, never a silent partial binding, never counted as a completed converted report; the measurement plan and the governed cap stay; a cap that refuses the required production workload FAILS the scale requirement; the storage decision follows the measured evidence (Codex / supervisor ruling). 3c-1 proceeds as authorised.

## 47. frps3c-1 — retained configuration evidence for every adapter + the balance family's consumed customer association / names + the named `BOUND_INPUT_MISSING` refusal (design §2.1 / §10.1 / §10.4; Codex fa64924e D1 / D4; docs first `44df0399`, code `8ede60b7`)

**Dispatch (supervisor: 3c-1 GO after the design review).** Configuration evidence for all TEN adapters PLUS D1's consumed customer names / association for the three balance-family builders (NULL vs absent), docs-first with the supervisor's numbers, fail-first CPU tests, DB-bound NOT RUN, lint-guarded, slot-2 gates (slot 1 = the integrated batch's), record, READY-for-review then READY. Design SHA-256 at this slice: `815ab24db8eaeb1dd3572aab2e6bab2925397616ef12a09cc6fb761349fc9e34`.

**Applied.** `tie_outs.entities(session, ids, params=)` / `calendars(session, found, params=)` / `book_of(session, params)`: a live build records the consumed entity rows (code, calendar_id, functional_currency, time_zone), every period row of each consumed calendar (id, key, name, fiscal_year, period_no, quarter_no, start, end as ISO dates) and the default book into `evidence.configuration` (`record_configuration` merges by id); a bound run returns exactly those (`bound_configuration`) and refuses by NAME an entity / calendar / book the binding does not hold (`missing_input` → `BOUND_INPUT_MISSING`, 409 `invalid-transition`, exact identities, never counts). `tie_outs.balances_at(..., params=)` selects `contract.customer_id`; a live build records the contract → customer association (`customer_of` → `evidence.customer_association`, a NULL customer retained as null) and the customer name (`label_for(CUSTOMER_NAME)`, a NULL name retained as null); a bound run resolves both from the binding — an absent contract key or customer key refuses by name — and never consumes the live `customer.name` (the outer join's value is discarded under a binding; a conditional select is the stricter alternative, open for the slice review). Entity codes: `tie_outs.entity_code_for` binds `labels.entity_code` in `rpo` (row construction), `revenue_waterfall`, `contract_history.population` (which also feeds `latest_contract_status` and the two legacy exports) and C4's `_entity_codes` (no live `legal_entity` read under a binding). Call sites pass `params=` (`rollforward.ranges`, `contract_balances`, `contract_history`, `revenue_waterfall`, `rpo`, C4). `framework.SOURCE_CONTRACTS`: `_CONFIG_OPEN` withdrawn from every adapter; `_CONFIGURATION` in every adapter's bound tuple; `_CUSTOMERS` in the balance family's. Live behaviour unchanged on the unbound path (the live reads are the same reads, recording what they consumed). No migration; `binding_version` stays 1 (additive kinds).

**Docs first (`44df0399`; Codex production-20260921-0316 on 81ac8be2 / 44df0399: the docs-first contracts "align with the previously accepted D1/D4 requirements"; the §10.5 over-cap wording is corrected — a named report failure when the required capture cannot complete, the scale requirement retained; "removal of the consumed-configuration open qualification is the contract for the forthcoming code, not evidence of an implemented reader at this head").** 04 T-RPT-02 (evidence kinds `configuration`, `customer_association`; label kinds `entity_code`, `customer_name`; the qualification withdrawn; `BOUND_INPUT_MISSING`), ENGINE_SPEC_B S15-R-24, dev-guide DG-AK-41 — numbers assigned by the supervisor per descending union; every row at its header's width. **Rule kept:** each `open` declaration is removed only by the ACTUAL complete reader — the qualification's removal in `44df0399` is the contract that the code commit `8ede60b7` discharges (the bound readers `entities` / `calendars` / `book_of` / `balances_at` / `entity_code_for` consume retained inputs; the fail-first run proves the contract was not yet implemented at the docs-first head).

**Tests.** Fail-first: `c31-fail-first.log` rc=2 on the docs-first tree (the new names absent); after the code `c31-unit-after.log` 21 passed rc=0. CPU (`tests/unit/reports/test_source_binding_configuration.py`): live recording of entities / calendars; bound reads with a session that refuses every query; named refusals for an absent entity / calendar / book / configuration kind; `book_of` bound; the customer association with NULL distinct from absent (bound and live); contracts (no adapter declares configuration open; the balance family binds customers; rpo / rpo_rollforward / waterfall / history declare nothing open). DB witnesses — ACCEPTANCE PATTERNS, not yet written as executable cases (Codex production-20260921-0550 correction of this line at landing; the implementation and native-evidence obligations stand: each pattern is still to be authored as a case in `tests/domain/reports/` and executed by the integrated batch — lane DB absent, Ray-side): a calendar reassignment / period edit after a balances run (bound rerun equal; a current run differs); a customer rename and a contract re-association after a balances run (bound rerun keeps the consumed name / association; current differs); a NULL customer name retained; a contract absent from the association → `BOUND_INPUT_MISSING`; the CTL-029 calculation-change mismatch preserved.

**Captured gates on `8594338c`** (the FINAL tree of this slice — `8ede60b7` + the -0304 fixture commit `31a81c82` + the -0354 correction `df7b3b61` + the typecheck fix `7da28840` + the -0414 container-shape correction `8594338c`; the intermediate gate runs on 8ede60b7 (`c31-*`; typecheck RED), df7b3b61 (`c31b-*`; stopped) and 7da28840 (`c31c-*`: typecheck 0 / lint 0 / focused 0 / docs 0 / CPU rc=1 (ten fixture errors) / selection 0) are superseded by these and never cited as this slice's evidence; CPU-only — no DB stage; PID-first slot claims: slot 1 claimed 21:17:28; slot released 21:18:05; slot 1 claimed 21:18:05; slot released 21:29:33. **Docs first `d23e146a`** (04 1.62 / ENGINE_SPEC_B 1.35 / dev-guide 1.54 — numbers assigned by the supervisor; rows appended at the table tails; headers as descending unions; the design's §8 row 3c-2 amended to STATE the contract re-association domain limit (supervisor 01:00 after Codex -0727 / -0734); prep line 1523 → frps3c-2 DISPATCHED 23:47). **Applied (code `259b7db1`).** `tie_outs.require_members(params, kind, found)` (D4): under a binding the rows a read obtained must be exactly the bound members — a bound id not returned refuses by name with the missing identities, never a count. `contract_balance_rollforward._flows` (feeds `contract_balances`), `contract_cost_rollforward._movements`, `revenue_from_opening_liability._activity` and `disaggregation`'s line read select the line id, carry `bound_member_where` BESIDE their tenant / book / entity / period / recorded_at predicates (the ids restrict within today's scope, never replace it), record the ids of the rows actually consumed (after each builder's own Python filters) as `members.subledger_line` on a live build and require them under a binding; the opening-liability aggregate stays an aggregate — its ids come from a companion select over the same predicates and the sum is re-computed from the bound rows (never a retained aggregate). `disaggregation` binds `product_family` / `product_revenue_category` per product id through `label_for` (never the live product join under a binding). `framework.SOURCE_CONTRACTS`: `_LINES_OPEN` withdrawn from the five contracts, `_LINES` (and disaggregation's label statement) added to their bound tuples — the five declare NOTHING open; the only adapter dimension still open is C4's subledger-line reference population (3c-3). Live behaviour on the unbound path unchanged. No migration; `binding_version` 1 (additive kind use).

**3c-1 DB acceptance cases AUTHORED** — `tests/domain/reports/test_source_binding_configuration_db.py` (6 cases; NOT RUN — lane database absent, Ray-side; the integrated batch measures): `test_an_entity_attribute_change_after_the_run_does_not_move_a_bound_rerun`, `test_a_calendar_that_grows_after_the_run_does_not_enter_a_bound_rerun`, `test_a_customer_rename_after_the_run_does_not_move_a_bound_rerun`, `test_a_binding_without_the_contracts_association_refuses_the_rerun_by_name`, `test_a_malformed_configuration_container_refuses_the_rerun_by_name`, `test_a_calculation_change_shows_as_a_ctl_029_mismatch_on_a_bound_balances_rerun`. ## 50. RPS-SNAP — the lock-snapshot dataset registry `erev_api.domain.reports.snapshots` (dispatch 08:0x on Codex production-20260921-1505 §3; design note first)

**Dispatch (supervisor, after the frps5 landing 97cca945 / d12e26fd merge of COMMIT 69):** the next substantive line is RPS-SNAP exactly as Codex 1505 §3 hands it off — the registry module is absent from the committed tree; RPS-4 / RPS-SNAP owes `SnapshotScope(entity_id, book_code, period_id, known_at)` and `SNAPSHOT_DATASETS`; `BUILDERS` has eighteen reports, ten corresponding to the twelve dataset kinds; `modification_register` and `manual_adjustment_register` have neither a source builder nor a builder module (producers F-CTR / CTR-17 and F-CLO) — no empty datasets as substitutes; `framework.create_run` requires `BUILDERS` membership; the CLO-8b → CLO-8c sequence and the public `test_lock.py:465–513` stand. Design note FIRST, before code.

**Design note:** `docs/reviews/loop/prod/PRODUCTION-F-RPS-RPS-SNAP-DESIGN.md` (decisions D1–D8: the agreed contract; ten adapters wrapping the real builders through `framework._resolve` + the catalogue schema; the two register kinds as a NAMED dependency refused by name — F-CLO's `freeze_datasets` keeps refusing the lock by name until both producers land; encoding = the engine's S15-R-18 `encode` over the report's canonical cell texts with the builder's control totals verbatim; no source binding for a frozen dataset; read side unchanged; open questions Q1–Q5; slice plan SNAP-1 / SNAP-2). Numbers for the 04 / ENGINE_SPEC_B / dev-guide rows on request.

**Amendment A1 (design note §11; docs-only commit after `02f11e7c`):** `framework._resolve` filters entities through the caller's `report.run` scope, and the PERIOD_LOCK decision runs under the approving Controller's principal — so the adapter normalises parameters like `_resolve` (defaults, `known_at` text, `historical` basis, closed-schema findings) but without the principal scope filter; `entity_ids = (scope.entity_id,)` by construction; a CPU parity witness pins equality with `_resolve` for an in-scope principal. The freeze's authorisation is the lock decision's, never a second report-permission check.

### 50.3 Codex production-20260921-1653 §1 / §2 RETURN SNAP-1 `13456566` — no READY sent; the §50.1 closing sentence superseded; A4 written

**Return (accepted, source-correct):** ID-1 — `snapshots.py:340` renders identity columns through `csv_output.cell` → `sanitise.guard`, so admitted ids `=A` and `'=A` freeze identically and `relock_diff._keyed` refuses the rebuilt tuple (identity loss, false re-lock refusal); ID-2 — the long-form row keys (`:453` / `:534`) join components with an unescaped `:`, so `(A:B, C)` and `(A, B:C)` collide and `Dataset` refuses distinct rows; DB-R1 — the authored DB module fixes `known_at` at 2026-09-30 while the inherited clock is 2026-09-12 and never advances (future-cutoff refusal before comparison; the JE late row never created later); DB-R2 — the frozen-byte witness requests JSON but asserts CSV identity; DB-R3 — the non-empty wrapper assertion lacks a journal population, and two September-active K01 obligations are claimed where O2 completes in February. Credits retained (ten adapters; the two named dependencies refused before any write; scope / selectors / defaults / historical wiring; `frozen_at` supported). §5: the A3 docs at `a9ffee27` are COMPLETE.

**Disclosure.** The §50.1 record (`f8079fa2`, 09:48:13) was written by the unattended chain before the return reached the lane and closes with "READY-for-review → RPS-SNAP SNAP-1 at 13456566". That sentence is SUPERSEDED here, not rewritten: `13456566` is RETURNED, no READY was sent, and none is sent before the A4 correction lands and is gate-measured. The b6 line (batch-#6 fix-forward `f01476fd`, record `c57dcdb8`) finished as designed — D-98 141 corrections only, its record stating the return and NOT READY.

## 51. READ-2 amended by dev-guide rev 1.219: the subledger read and the blocks that wait for a close run (lane QA-BE, 2026-10-01)

Recorded on the supervisor's order of 2026-10-01, so that the rule's text in this record points at its amendment. Rule READ-2 is stated in §19 and amended in §21 and §24 to §28; those sections stand as written. Dev-guide rev 1.219 (DG-AK-41; register index 142; the supervisor's rulings of 2026-10-01 on the runner's reads and its world, (a) and (b); rulings log R-122 (j)) amends the rule once more and is the text that governs. Nothing is restated here:

- §19 rebuilt the posting intents of a checkpoint book from the as-of version's `subledger_posting` alone (same `contract_computation_id` and book). Since rev 1.219 they are the group's sealed postings through the cutoff — the posting of every computation of the group known at or before `known_at[after_seq]`, each placed by its computation's `known_at` — because the product posts each computation's increments;
- a block whose expected figures hold a period-end pass is not run by name and not compared until the plan runs the product's close run (`platform_runner.close_pass_blocks`; item AK-CLOSE-RUN-STEP-1).

Code and tests: `workspace_reads.PersistedReads._intents` and `output_bundle`, `platform_runner.close_pass_blocks` and their unit modules; lane head `94864f3d`, on main as `cd302602`.
